  - (owner decision 2026-10-19) In M1, engine environments are recorded and not restored by the suite. `lil doctor` compares the running ComfyUI and its custom nodes with the last capture and, when they changed, says what changed and how to roll back with ComfyUI's own tools. A suite-driven restore is CORE-166 (Later). Each capture is kept in the suite `state.db`, the newest 10 per engine, and `engines.environment` compares the newest two (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
  - (owner decision 2026-10-20; plan 6a) **Model hashes in exported workflows:** every model entry the suite writes into a workflow it exports or saves (IMG-139, APP-080) carries `hash` and `hash_type: "sha256"`, so ComfyUI's missing-models dialog can find the file.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) The output collector takes every save node's outputs, whether image, mesh, audio or video, into the staging handle, or records that it doesn't.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-20; a plan 6a spike question) **Preview frames and the websocket handshake.** Appendix F has the core never announce `supports_preview_metadata`, so ComfyUI sends it plain preview frames, which carry no prompt id, and ComfyUI broadcasts progress and previews of a prompt submitted without a `client_id`. Plan 6a's spike checks, on the pinned ComfyUI, whether announcing the flag gives preview frames tagged with their prompt id.
    - If it does, the core announces it first in every connection and uses only frames tagged with a suite `prompt_id`; that replaces Appendix F's "never announces" line.
    - If it doesn't, Appendix F's rule stays, and a plain preview frame is published only while a suite prompt is the one executing (from the `executing` events), so a frame from an outside script job is dropped.
    - Either way, a reconnect resubscribes with the same `clientId` (Appendix F's one id per channel).
- **CORE-131** [M1] Model downloads are core jobs with a disk-space check. Custom-node pack installs are `exec` and need a ComfyUI restart, with a warning about running jobs. (owner decision 2026-10-20) Downloads become resumable install jobs (CORE-165).
- **CORE-164** [M1] (contract) **Model inventory report** (owner decision 2026-10-20; plan 4a). One glossary, shared with workspace files (ARCH-079, CORE-038): a model file is **missing** when `registry/models.yaml` records it and it isn't on disk, **orphan** when it is on disk and unrecorded, **duplicate** when two files have one SHA-256, and **unknown_family** when no model family can be read from the file's header metadata (the safetensors or GGUF header, which plan 4a reads); IMG-109's tensor-key fingerprint for LoRAs refines this from M1b. `models.report {}` (`read`) returns the inventory (CORE-130) with these states, and Diagnostics and `lil doctor` show it. Nothing is deleted or moved because of a state. The family is read only from `.safetensors`, `.sft` and `.gguf` headers, within Appendix G's size and key limits, and pickle formats are never parsed: they are opened only to hash them (CORE-130) (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G; plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24: "never parsed" replaces "never opened").
- **CORE-165** [M1] (contract) **Model installs and cards** (owner decision 2026-10-20; plan 6a, with downloads):
  - a model download is an install job you can pause, resume and restart file by file; a paused job keeps its partial files in staging;
  - a model card shows a local cover (the first output made with the model, never fetched from a hub, ARCH-122), your notes, trigger words, and "used by N recipes" from the index;
  - starter bundles show their total size before you choose them (IMG-105).
- **CORE-166** [Later] (contract) **Engine snapshot restore** (owner decision 2026-10-19: not before Later). The suite lists the recorded engine environments (CORE-130) and restores one through ComfyUI's own tools (ComfyUI-Manager snapshots, comfy-cli's version switch). A restore is `exec`, and the suite asks you before each one, whoever starts it.
- **CORE-179** [M1] (contract) **ComfyUI inbox** (owner decision 2026-10-21; plan 4b). The core watches the owned ComfyUI's `output` folder (ARCH-065, ARCH-126). Each new image there lands in the workspace inbox (`inbox/<import_id>/`, workspace format I1) with its prompt, model and seed read from the file's embedded metadata as untrusted, size-limited input, as recall reads a dropped ComfyUI PNG (IMG-163, ARCH-060).
  - You review it in the inbox and can keep it as an asset through `asset.import` (APP-073). Nothing becomes an asset until you keep it.
  - The watcher is on by default; a setting turns it off.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 4b) The inbox lists every new output file, typed or not, not only images; a file whose kind has no renderer shows its type icon (APP-038).

### 2.9 QA and repair

- **CORE-132** [M1] (contract) QA gates are technical and data-driven per asset type, overridable per project, plus checks that the recipe and environment are recorded and the inputs resolve. The suite adds no IP screen and no licence gate of its own: the user is responsible for the licences of the models and inputs they use, and each provider's and model's own terms apply. (owner decision 2026-10-20) The suite does record each model's licence and, in a project marked for release, warns about licence problems at export; the warnings are information only and never block (CORE-174). (owner decision 2026-10-21) Technical gates block. Here that means a candidate that fails a technical gate is never chosen by automatic sign-off (CORE-051, CORE-052), and an agent's `asset.signoff` of it (any caller other than `ui:*`) fails with `qa.gate_failed`, naming the gates; the failure is shown with its reason. Your own sign-off from an app is never blocked: it overrides the failed gates, and the override is logged (CORE-051).
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) **Gates are data.** Gates are data over named metrics, keyed by the type string in the QA profile. A verdict of `not_run` or `error` never counts as a pass: a gate whose verdict isn't `pass` counts as failed for automatic and agent sign-off. A type with no QA profile gets the shared checks plus a "no type gates" warning.
- **CORE-172** [M1] (decision) **QA and repair on every asset** (owner decisions 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). Every candidate run goes through QA and repair automatically (CORE-025). (owner decision 2026-10-21) Every asset is checked automatically before you see it. Ruling (plan-writer, 2026-10-21): staged results count as "before you see it", so each proposal (IMG-079) is checked before the staging bar shows it: the fast technical checks and the style or identity score run and are shown on it, deterministic repairs run, and the critique-and-repair loop runs according to its mode (CORE-173). These checks are stored with the proposal run, not in `qa/`, and they don't make it a candidate run or change the asset's status (IMG-080). Live-mode results (IMG-161), which are not proposal runs, are not checked (placed by plan-writer reading, 2026-10-21). There are three kinds of check:
  - **technical gates** block (CORE-132), with per-project overrides;
  - **style and identity checks** only warn. On a failed check the suite tries one repair, and the warning gives the reason. They start in M1b, when the style and character kits they measure against exist (placed by plan-writer reading, 2026-10-21);
  - the **critique-and-repair loop** (CORE-173).

  Every change a repair makes, deterministic or generated, carries its reason and a before-and-after view, and the original is kept (CORE-135).
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) **Repairs to your own takes are offered, not applied.** The same checks run on every run. For a run you made (role `manual`, or a version from a human edit, CORE-034), deterministic and model repairs are written as an extra take labelled "repaired by QA", with its reason and its before-and-after; your take stays the main one, shown first. The critique loop (CORE-173) runs on your runs only when you ask, and automatic sign-off never prefers the repaired take (CORE-052).
- **CORE-173** [M1b] (contract) **Critique-and-repair loop** (owner decision 2026-10-21). A critique names what fails in a candidate, and a repair changes only that; then the candidate is checked again. Its mode is set per project in the settings cascade (CORE-057) and per asset in its manifest, the asset's value winning:
  - `rounds_3` (the default): up to 3 rounds, stopping early when a round improves nothing;
  - `rounds_1`: one round;
  - `off`: the first output is returned as it is. Technical gates still apply, and a failed style or identity check still shows its warning but makes no repair.

  The mode governs only model-driven critique and repair. The deterministic fixes of the completion chain (CORE-025, CORE-135, such as CORE-136's alpha snap or a format fix) run in every mode, `off` included. A failed style or identity check gets one repair attempt in total (CORE-172), made in the first round, so it adds no round of its own. Each round is metered and reserves against caps like any call (CORE-086), and shows its before and after with the reason.
- **CORE-133** [M1] **Runners:** native Rust image measurements (dimensions, alpha histogram, colour count, pixel-grid estimate, perceptual hash), and an optional vision review through `ProviderGate` when a provider is configured, metered like any call.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) **One runner contract.** A runner's kind is `native`, `engine`, `model` or `tool`; a `tool` runner runs out of process as a job, and plan 6a only declares that kind. Plan 6a builds only the image runners above.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) The vision-review and critique-judge routes are resolved through the project's settings cascade (`defaults.models.vision_qa` and a judge key, AI-036, CORE-057), never from a suite-wide default, so a project that generates locally never sends its outputs to a hosted judge without saying so.
- **CORE-134** [M1] (contract) QA output goes to `qa/<asset_id>/…/report.md` and `report.json`, summarised in the manifest's `qa` block.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) **A report for any type.** `report.json` names its `subject: {kind, ref}`; plan 6a writes the kinds `candidate_run` and `proposal_run`, and the kind is an open string. Every check entry has one shape: `{check_id, class: gate | warning | advisory, runner: {kind, id, version}, verdict: pass | fail | not_run | error, severity?, reason, evidence[], witness?}` (runner kinds: CORE-133). Model verdicts go in `judgements[]`. Each report records the hash of the QA profile it ran. `qa.run` takes `{subject, checks?}`.
- **CORE-135** [M1] (contract) **Repair loop:** detect → classify → deterministic fix → re-check. After 2 failed passes it proposes a narrow regeneration, then shows you the evidence. Originals are kept and repairs are logged. (owner decision 2026-10-20) Each logged repair records what changed and why, and Review shows its before and after (CORE-172).
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) Playbook entries are registered as `{id, version, types, detects, fix, verify}`, and a repair keeps its before and after as evidence references in the report (CORE-134), not as image paths.
- **CORE-136** [M1] The first playbook entry snaps cut-out alpha 254 → 255.

### 2.10 Convert

- **CORE-137** [M1] (decision) The LILITH document (folder or `.lildoc`, §3.5) is the native layered format. OpenRaster (`.ora`) is import (best effort) and export (lossy, with a warning listing what is lost) only.
  - Why: ORA can't hold the document losslessly: an export bakes masks into alpha, rasterises transforms, approximates blend modes outside ORA's 20 and reduces float depth to 16-bit (APP-060).
- **CORE-138** [M1a] PNG, JPEG and WebP (lossless via `image`, lossy via libwebp), with ICC handling via `moxcms`. (owner decision 2026-10-19) Imported images follow the colour policy of IMG-169.
- **CORE-139** [M1b] TIFF (8- and 16-bit) and OpenRaster import/export (hand-rolled on `zip`). (owner decision 2026-10-19) Also layered PSD export (IMG-137), for handing work to Krita or Photopea; ORA stays.
- **CORE-140** [Later] EXR (`exr`), PSD import out of process (`psd-tools` in the QA venv), KRA, glTF/USD/FBX, WAV/FLAC/OGG. (owner decision 2026-10-19) PSD export moves to M1b (CORE-139); PSD import stays here. HEIC and AVIF decoders are added here too, behind the untrusted-decoder isolation (CORE-141).
- **CORE-141** [M1] (invariant) Decoders for untrusted files run with size limits and panic isolation, or out of process.
- **CORE-142** [M1] ComfyUI's PNGs embed the prompt and workflow in text chunks; export offers to keep or strip them. (owner decision 2026-10-19) Export also strips GPS position and camera serial numbers by default (IMG-136). Keeping camera data is one opt-in, and keeping GPS position is a second, separate one.

### 2.11 Tools

- **CORE-143** [M1] (contract) Regeneration tools take an explicit `scope`, write `partial_edits[]`, skip locked or human-edited files, and return a before/after comparison.
- **CORE-144** [M1] (contract) Every `image.*` tool in CORE-146 takes a `target` (`doc:<id>` plus frame, or an asset) and a `destination` (`proposals`, `asset` or `both`), plus the generation settings the Generate panel has (§5.5), and an optional Prompt Studio `compile_id` with `overrides` (IMG-176). Their results are JSON text that includes the file paths of rendered PNGs of the frame and of the proposals (CORE-022).
- **CORE-145** [M1] (invariant) `image.inpaint` and `image.outpaint` composite the result back so that everything outside the mask is unchanged, and verify it.
- **CORE-146** [M1] (contract) The core exposes these tools, with these effect classes (§2.7):

| Tool | Effect | Milestone | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image.generate` | write | M1a | N candidates or proposals |
| `image.regenerate` | write | M1a | variations: re-runs a staged result's or candidate's recipe with new seeds, optionally with only the prompt changed (§5.5) |
| `image.inpaint`, `image.outpaint` | write | M1a | |
| `image.select_object` | write | M1a | SAM through ComfyUI |
| `image.preprocess` | write | M1a | control-layer preprocessors |
| `image.cutout` | write | M1a | BiRefNet: remove background; select subject / select background return a selection instead of a cut-out layer (§5.5) |
| `image.remove` | write | M1a | the Remove brush, an interactive job (IMG-165, CORE-160) |
| `image.edit` | write | M1b | instruction edit (Qwen-Image, FLUX.2 klein) |
| `image.upscale` | write | M1b | upscale image, enhance layer, upscale document; Real-ESRGAN, SeedVR2 (§5.5) |
| `image.workflow.run` | write | M1b | runs a saved, sent or layer workflow (any graph); labelled a full regeneration (§5.5, §5.7) |
| `doc.layer.*` (add, properties, reorder, merge, delete), `doc.frame.set`, `doc.selection.*`, `doc.mask.from_selection`, `doc.references.*`, `doc.proposals.accept`, `doc.proposals.discard`, `doc.export`, `doc.make_candidate` | write | M1a | applied to the open document as one undoable step (agent calls are forwarded to the app holding it, §3.5), or by the core on closed documents under its own hold |
| `doc.get_snapshot`, `doc.list_open`, `doc.proposals.list` | read | M1a | |
| `asset.signoff`, `asset.request_signoff`, `asset.pin_input`, `asset.transfer`, `asset.import`, `asset.restore_version` | write | M1 | `asset.import` copies or links external; `asset.restore_version`: CORE-177 |
| `assets.query`, `asset.get` | read | M1 | `assets.query` filters, facets and duplicates: CORE-152, CORE-155, CORE-168 |
| `asset.mark`, `asset.rename`, `asset.move`, `asset.make_independent`, `asset.accept_move` | write | M1 | marks (CORE-152); rename and move with a redirect (CORE-153, ARCH-125); independent copy and accepting an outside move (CORE-154) |
| `asset.trash` | destructive | M1 | the whole asset, sealed versions included; asks every caller, listing dependents (CORE-157) |
| `asset.deprecate` | write | M1 | reversible; files stay and dependents keep working (CORE-170) |
| `collections.list` | read | M1 | saved searches and collections (CORE-155) |
| `collection.save` | write | M1 | |
| `collection.delete` | destructive | M1 | soft delete (CORE-155) |
| `project.create`, `project.set_status` | write | M1 | |
| `project.status`, `project.context` | read | M1 | |
| `projects.list`, `workspaces.list` | read | M1 | the top bar's switchers (APP-006); switching workspace is a new `core/hello` (ARCH-030) |
| `resource.save` | write | M1 | |
| `resource.list`, `resource.get` | read | M1 | |
| `resource.delete` | destructive | M1 | soft delete |
| `qa.run`, `convert.export` | write | M1 | `qa.run {subject, checks?}` (CORE-134; owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6a) |
| `job.cancel`, `job.retry`, `diagnostics.bundle` | write | M1 | `job.cancel` by ids, batch or all of the caller's (CORE-160) |
| `job.move` | write | M1 | next or last within the job's class (CORE-160) |
| `job.estimate`, `models.report` | read | M1 | the estimator (CORE-161); the model inventory states (CORE-164) |
| `engines.environment`, `diagnostics.recent` | read | M1 | the newest two engine captures compared (CORE-130); recent errors by code (ARCH-121) |
| `image.stage_settings` | write | M1a | fills a labelled settings card without running (IMG-164) |
| `jobs.list`, `job.wait`, `engines.status` | read | M1 | `job.wait` waits ≤ ~90 s per call (§4) |
| `engines.start` | write | M1 | |
| `engines.stop`, `engines.restart` | destructive | M1 | can kill outside jobs; refused for an attached engine |
| `engines.comfyui.install_node` | exec | M1 | pinned version; needs a ComfyUI restart |
| `usage.summary`, `caps.get`, `accounts.list` | read | M1 | caps are changed only through `app/caps.set` |
| `accounts.refresh` | write | M1 | |
| `telemetry.series`, `telemetry.snapshot`, `telemetry.jobs` | read | M1 | |

| `lilref.write`, `notify.claude_code` | write | M1 | a `.lilref` file in the cache for a sealed ref (APP-021); Claude Code's notices as toasts, from `lil hook notification` (APP-146) |
| `prompt.interview_start`, `prompt.interview_answer`, `brief.create`, `brief.update`, `brief.save_version`, `brief.compile`, `brief.stage_to`, `brief.promote` | write | M1a | Prompt Studio (§4.7a, AI-187); `brief.stage_to` is fill-only (AI-186) |
| `prompt.interview_get`, `prompt.interviews_list`, `prompt.dialects`, `brief.get`, `briefs.list` | read | M1a | `prompt.interviews_list`: AI-187 (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 6b) |
| `project.clone` | write | Later | |
| `project.delete` | destructive | Later | lists inbound links first |

The plan-4a rows' arguments, results and modules (`projects.list` in `projects`, `workspaces.list` in `workspace`, `desktop.get` in `desktop`, `engines.environment` in `engines`, `telemetry.energy` in `telemetry`, `diagnostics.recent` in `diagnostics`, `lilref.write` and `notify.claude_code` in `interchange`) are fixed in Appendix G (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).

**Example — "generate 3 lantern candidates" from Claude Code:** resolve (caller `mcp`, project `sketchbook`) → validate → settings → estimate $0 (local) → no confirmation or cap prompt → job in the `gpu-heavy` lane with core-minted prompt ids, the three prompts submitted one after another as each finishes → `/history` + `/view` → `candidates/<run_id>/` → QA → meter ($0; about 20 s of GPU time at up to 360 W ≈ 2 Wh GPU, ~3 Wh whole system) → run recorded → events. The Image studio shows the candidates live; Claude Code gets JSON text with the file paths and a hand-off note.

### 2.12 Workspace format, registries and the `worldwright` plugin

- **CORE-178** [M1] (contract) The workspace format's reference text is `docs/reference/workspace-format.md` (from the `worldwright` plugin's manifest skill). Where this spec (this section and Appendix E) differs from it, the spec takes precedence.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 4b) **Reserved names.** Plan 4b's update of the reference marks these as reserved and not read by the core: I4's `engine`, `engine_project_path`, `platforms` and `vcs`, with the values no plan has chosen yet; I4's `pipeline.extra_reviews`, and stage IDs the core has no stage for (CORE-054), whose behaviour isn't specified yet; and I3's `plan/assets.csv`, `plan/schedule.md` and `plan/open-questions.md`. `leases` is a reserved manifest extension name (CORE-036). The asset type names `feature`, `level` and `system`, and the field name `code_ref`, are reserved too.
- **CORE-147** [M1] (contract) New workspace and asset files no longer get `mode`, `commercial`, `territories`, `content_rating`, `privacy_tier`, `provider_allowlist`, `budget_usd`, licence-gate or disclosure fields. Asset manifests no longer get `license_scope`, `commercial_use`, `content_rating`, or the licence/C2PA/disclosure parts of `provenance`; the recipe, environment and inputs stay. (owner decision 2026-10-20) The ban on gate fields stays, with no commercial or territory gates. Two information-only licence fields are added (CORE-174): the project's `for_release` switch, and the licence note on model registry entries, hosted routes and profiles, which each recipe copies. Neither gates anything, and no disclosure field is stored (CORE-175).
- **CORE-174** [M1] (contract) **Licence record and release warnings** (owner decisions 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21).
  - Each model file entry in `registry/models.yaml` (AI-034) and each model profile (IMG-099) carries a `licence` note: the licence's name, its source link, the date it was read, and plain flags such as `non_commercial` or `territory_limits` with a one-line summary. An entry whose licence hasn't been read says `unknown`.
  - A hosted route (`routes[]` in AI-034, and the hosted profile kind of AI-033) carries the same note for the provider's model terms as recorded in `models.yaml`, flagging what they say about using the output; `unknown` when they aren't recorded.
  - Every asset records which model made it: the recipe already names the profile and model hashes (IMG-140), and it also copies the licence note, of the model file or of the hosted route, as it stood at generation time.
  - `project.yaml` has a `for_release` switch, off by default. In a project with it on, export (`convert.export`, `doc.export`) shows a licence warning for each asset whose recipe names a model with a `non_commercial`, `territory_limits` or `unknown` licence. The warning is information only and never blocks the export.
  - An asset moved or transferred into a `for_release` project (`asset.move`, `asset.transfer`) is checked again, and the result names the assets that would warn.
  - The licence fields come with plan 6a's model profiles, and the hosted-route notes with plan 6b's routes.
- **CORE-175** [Later] **Release report** (owner decisions 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21; with the 2D studio's export). On demand, the report lists the AI use of each asset in a project: which files are of AI origin, the models and licences used, and the hand-edited share. It can draft plain-text store disclosure wording from that, which you copy. Nothing is stored or submitted anywhere, and responsibility for the wording stays with you.
- **CORE-148** [M1] (invariant) Fields the suite no longer uses are ignored and preserved when files are rewritten (lossless round-trip).
- **CORE-149** [M1] New optional workspace fields hold default model and provider preferences per task.
- **CORE-150** [M1] (contract) **Registries:** workspace `registry/models.yaml` registers model files (sources, hashes, settings, routes, fallbacks); personal `model_profile` resources refer to model files by hash; `defaults.models.image_generate` names a profile. `providers.yaml` (endpoints, billing basis, account-API capabilities) and `prices.yaml` are suite-level (§2.6). `licenses.yaml` is no longer used, and `ledger.csv` is replaced by the usage logs.
- **CORE-151** [M1] (decision) **Plugin 1.3** — the suite's Claude Code plugin, with the MCP server and hooks, shipped with the suite — replaces the current 1.2.0 file-based plugin and is rewritten for a personal hobby suite:
  - not carried over: modes, licence gates, the plugin's own content-rule checks, approval thresholds, store disclosures and compliance workflows; the `licenses` and `compliance` skills and the `license-auditor` agent are retired;
  - kept: projects, versions and candidates, recipes, QA and repair, the library, the dependency graph, routing, usage and cost tracking and caps, agent safety confirmations (including the hooks in §2.7), and the suite's MCP tools.

### 2.13 Milestone scope

- **M1:** every module in §2.1 except `imports` and multi-machine `sync`, with: the dispatcher; assets, candidates, sign-off, trash and proposals; content hashes in references; cap reservations in the suite-level `state.db`; creation UUIDs on every asset; the status machine and settings cascade; telemetry with per-job energy; usage records for every provider, cost sources, M1 price rules and the reprice job; optional caps; fixed recurring costs; account sync for Anthropic (headers), OpenRouter, NanoGPT and Claude Code plan usage per profile; agent safety confirmations; the suite-held ComfyUI queue with its class order, batches, run next/last and Pause GPU work (CORE-160); QA and repair; organising marks, saved searches and collections, the status log, rename and move with redirects, moved and copied folder repair, and the duplicate finder (CORE-152–156, CORE-168, ARCH-125); trashing a whole asset, which asks every caller and lists its dependents (CORE-157), and deprecating one (CORE-170); the estimator (CORE-161); the job-record fields and the stall rule (CORE-117, ARCH-072); the out-of-memory ladder (CORE-163); the owned-engine launch profile (ARCH-126); the model inventory report, resumable model installs and model cards (CORE-164, CORE-165); included routes with their simulated cost (CORE-171); QA and repair on every asset (CORE-172), with the critique-and-repair loop modes in M1b (CORE-173); licence notes and release warnings (CORE-174); the pick log (CORE-176); the converters and tools tagged M1, M1a or M1b in §2.10–§2.11 (M1a/M1b follow §5's split).
- **M1.5:** the `network` lane and hosted media jobs (fal account sync with them), except what plan 6b's direct image APIs need (AI-033); local LLM engines beyond ORACLE's chat-only use (which is M1b, AI-204); the loopback OpenTelemetry receiver for interactive Claude Code sessions; document checkpoints (cloud store `docs/` prefix); large-file store changes involving a cloud store; destinations other than the local drive and OneDrive; multi-machine sync with UUID-based collision renames and resolution through `renamed_from`.
- **Later:** checkout leases; `project.clone`, `project.delete`, `portfolio.status`; `imports`; `lib:` links and promote; further price-rule kinds; account sync for further providers and admin-key reports; the later converters; engine snapshot restore (CORE-166); relinking outside files by hash (CORE-158); a derived-data cache (CORE-159); re-applying read-only after every git rescan (CORE-169); the release report, with the 2D studio (CORE-175).

---

## 3. Core ↔ app interface, interchange, documents and dashboards

This section covers what every app is made of, how apps talk to the core, how apps and outside programs exchange work, the LILITH document (the suite's native editable format), and the dashboards. In M1 the dashboards are panels inside the Image studio; a separate Hub app comes later. Usage tracking, optional caps, account info, hardware telemetry and agent safety confirmations are all shown here; nothing in this section filters content or gates work on business rules.

The core modules behind this section are `docs` (document bundles, `.lildoc`, holds, save handles, snapshots, GC, checkpoints), `resources` (Libraries) and `interchange` (handler registry, `open_in`, `ui.show`, `.lilref`, notifications, thumbnails); see §2.1.

### 3.1 What every app is made of

- **APP-001** [M1] (decision) **One app = one process, one QML engine, one or more windows.** Windows use KDDockWidgets 2.4.1 (Qt Quick) for dockable, floatable and tabbable panels, themed through its ViewFactory QML (verified 2026-10-12). (owner decision 2026-10-19) 2.5.0 if its build check passes (APP-005). (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: 2.5.0 passed its build check (S0.1), so windows use KDDockWidgets 2.5.0. `docs/spikes/10-app-shell.md`.)
- **APP-002** [M1] (contract) **Layout preset format:** an app-level JSON file holding the list of main windows (unique name, normal geometry, screen name and scale) plus the KDDockWidgets layout blob.
- **APP-003** [M1] (invariant) **Layout restore order:** create the windows → register the dock factory → restore → clamp every window to the current screen's available area. The clamp works around known mixed-DPI and maximized-restore bugs.
- **APP-004** [M1] M1 keeps the "Default" layout plus the last state. Named layout presets arrive in M1.5.
- **APP-005** [M1] (decision) Upgrade to KDDockWidgets 2.5 (per-window save, Qt Quick dark mode) when it ships. (owner decision 2026-10-19: was [Later]; 2.5.0 has shipped, so the trigger has fired.) **KDDockWidgets 2.5.0** replaces 2.4.1 if a half-day build check early in plan 4a passes: the shim, the dock factory and layout save and restore (APP-002, APP-003) build and run against Qt 6.12.0. Whether 2.5.0 supports Qt 6.12.0 is unverified until that check. If it fails, the suite stays on 2.4.1. (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: KDDockWidgets 2.5.0 passed the build check on Qt 6.12.0 (S0.1: configure and build exit 0 with 0 warning lines from our files, the themed dock factory, 3 of 3 layout save-and-restore cycles with every window back at its saved geometry within 2 px and every dock in place, and the per-window save), so it replaces 2.4.1, which isn't needed. `docs/spikes/10-app-shell.md`.)
- **APP-161** [M1] (decision) **The asset-generator loop is the product's core** (owner decisions 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). In every studio you (1) describe the asset in text, (2) get candidates and (3) revise, for as many rounds as you like. Each round is a new proposal run or candidate run on the same asset (IMG-079, CORE-023, CORE-030), and there is no round limit. The loop is built from these existing rules, not new ones:
  - **describe:** the prompt box and Prompt Studio (IMG-176, §4.7a);
  - **candidates:** the staging bar (IMG-086) and Review (APP-112);
  - **text feedback:** change the prompt and run variations (IMG-075), or an instruction edit that says what to change and what to keep (IMG-071, IMG-179);
  - **region marking:** a selection or inpaint mask, so only that region is regenerated and everything outside it stays exact (IMG-018, IMG-062, IMG-067, CORE-143);
  - **reference images:** IMG-019;
  - **version history with side-by-side compare:** sealed versions (CORE-026) in the Inspector, and Compare for candidates and for two sealed versions (APP-141, APP-113), both from plan 4b;
  - **branching from any version:** a new run or document from any sealed version, which reopens exactly as it was (CORE-028, CORE-030), and "restore as new version" (APP-113, CORE-177);
  - **mixing parts of two versions:** a second version placed as a layer in the document, with a mask keeping the part you want (APP-075, IMG-012).

  Each later studio maps its own operations onto the same loop when its plan is written.
- **APP-163** [M1] **Takes** (owner decision 2026-10-21). In user-facing copy each candidate is a **take**, numbered Take 1, Take 2 and so on and grouped by revision round (APP-161). "Version" in copy means only a signed-off, sealed result (CORE-026). "Candidate" stays the spec's and the code's word; this rule changes copy only.
- **APP-164** [M1] **Studio names** (owner decision 2026-10-21). Each studio has a proper name: ATHANOR, the Image studio; ALEMBIC, the 2D studio; CRUCIBLE, the 3D studio; AETHER, the Audio studio; MERCURIUS, the Animation studio; TERRA, the World studio; GRIMOIRE, the Narrative studio; SPECULUM, the Video studio; HOMUNCULUS, the Avatar studio.
  - The proper name goes in the studio's window title and on its splash. Launcher tiles and menus lead with the plain name ("3D studio") and show the proper name beneath it. (owner decision 2026-10-22) Until the Image studio exists, its window is titled `LILITH — <project>`.
  - The shell is simply LILITH, with no proper name of its own; the Asset browser and Review are panels in it, not studios.

**Shared shell** (`lil-ui-kit`), identical in every app:

- **APP-006** [M1] **Top bar:** workspace/project switcher, app switcher, assistant toggle, and status indicators for jobs, spend this month vs caps, GPU load/VRAM/temperature, and the core connection.
- **APP-007** [Later] **Command palette** (Ctrl+K) in the top bar: assets, resources, tools, panels, recent files.
- **APP-008** [M1] **Shared panels:** Home, Asset browser, Inspector, Review, Libraries, Jobs, Confirmations, Usage & spend, Hardware, Backups (opened from Diagnostics in M1, APP-133), Assistant, and Diagnostics (with the event log). The Image studio also hosts its own panels (§5.1) and the ComfyUI web view, which is loaded lazily on first show (APP-137).
- **APP-009** [M1a] (invariant) The embedded ComfyUI web view is locked down as in §1.4 and §5.8: it loads ComfyUI from its own loopback origin, navigation is pinned to that origin, **downloads are blocked**, and there is no bridge from the page into the suite.
- **APP-010** [M1] **Shared dialogs:**
  - "Open from LILITH": search, type filter, version or take, and "Loose file…" which opens the native file dialog;
  - "Place / use as input": linked or embedded;
  - "Save to project": project (default `sketchbook`), asset name and type.
- **APP-011** [M1] **One shortcut map** for the whole suite. App-specific hotkeys (§5.1) are checked against it for conflicts. (owner decision 2026-10-19) The map is scoped by the focused panel (APP-143).
- **APP-143** [M1] (contract) **Scoped shortcuts** (owner decision 2026-10-19). The shortcut map has global keys plus one scope per kind of panel, and the focused panel picks the scope, so a letter can mean different things in different panels:
  - **browser and Review scope:** G grid, E loupe, C compare (APP-141), N survey (APP-142), Space quick look (the selected item fills the panel while Space is held); P pick, X reject, U unflag, with Shift+P and Shift+X setting the flag and moving to the next item; 1–5 rate and 0 clears; 6–9 set the labels red, yellow, green and blue (purple has no key); B adds to or removes from the quick collection (CORE-155);
  - **canvas scope:** IMG-008's keys, plus Q for quick-mask painting (IMG-022);
  - the conflict check covers each scope together with the global keys.
- **APP-012** [M1] **Theme:** dark, built from design tokens. A light theme comes later.
- **APP-156** [M1] **Accessibility, minimal** (owner decision 2026-10-19). Every `lil-ui-kit` control has an accessible role and name, and can be reached and used from the keyboard. Custom delegates (grid tiles, gauges, in-app notification cards) set their role and name themselves. A Narrator smoke check is on each milestone's manual check list (QA-064). There is no formal accessibility audit. Whether Narrator reads KDDockWidgets' title bars and tabs is unverified; plan 4a's first smoke check records it, and any gap goes to the owner. (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: all 5 items passed (S0.7), read through UI Automation, the provider data Narrator reads, instead of a Narrator walk-through (owner direction 2026-10-23), so tab order and what Narrator speaks are not tested: a KDDockWidgets title bar, a dock tab and a named button were exposed with their names, an `Accessible.announce()` message arrived as a UIA notification, and a custom chart tile was exposed as ControlType.Custom, so its accessible name must carry the word "chart". No item failed, so nothing goes to the owner. `docs/spikes/10-app-shell.md`.)

**Single instance and standalone launch**

- **APP-013** [M1] (contract) **Single instance per app, independent of the core:** each app owns a per-user named pipe `\\.\pipe\lilith-app-<app>-<userSID>-<channel>`, created with `first_pipe_instance` and the same user-only security descriptor as the core pipe (§1.2).
- **APP-014** [M1] A second launch of an app (Start menu, Explorer double-click, `lil-image <file-or-ref>`) connects to that pipe, reads the running instance's process id, calls `AllowSetForegroundWindow(pid)`, passes its arguments, and exits. The running instance then activates its window.
- **APP-015** [M1] **Standalone launch:** the app uses the default workspace from `suite.toml`; with no project chosen, work lands in `sketchbook`.
- **APP-016** [M1] **No-workspace state** (no workspace at all): a banner with "Create or choose a workspace"; the Asset browser is replaced by a list of recent loose files; "Add to project" is disabled; metered calls are still allowed and logged as loose usage (§2.0).

**Windows integration**

- **APP-017** [M1a] **Per-user file associations** and "Open with LILITH Image" for PNG, JPEG, WebP and `.lildoc`, registered under `HKCU\Software\Classes` (no admin). The suite never sets itself as the default; you choose defaults yourself.
- **APP-018** [M1b] TIFF and ORA join the file associations when their import and export land (§2.10, §5.11); before that the suite registers no association for a format it can't read.
- **APP-019** [M1] A file opened from Explorer that lives inside a workspace opens as that asset. Sealed files open read-only, with "Edit → new working document".
- **APP-020** [M1] (contract) **`lil:` URL protocol:** `lil:open/<ref>`, `lil:confirm/<id>` and `lil:task/<job_id>` (opens a director task in the Assistant panel, APP-050). Arguments are validated as untrusted input. It's used for notification clicks and for links in browsers and Markdown. (owner decision 2026-10-19) Asset refs (`lil://…`, ARCH-042) share the scheme, so a browser that opens one launches the same handler. The handler classifies every argument explicitly as `lil:open/<ref>`, `lil:confirm/<id>`, `lil:task/<job_id>` or a `lil://` asset ref, which it opens as `lil:open/<ref>` would. Anything else is refused with a message, never guessed. An activation never performs an irreversible action by itself (APP-022). Each channel has its own activation scheme (`lil`, `lil-dev`, `lil-test-<id>`), the verbs `confirm/`, `deny/`, `task/` and `open/` (whose target is base64url) are accepted only under the channel's own scheme, a raw `lil://` ref is accepted under every channel, and in plan 4a `task` opens the Jobs panel until the Assistant panel exists (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
- **APP-021** [M1] **`.lilref` pointer files:** links printed in Claude Code output point to a small `.lilref` file (associated with the suite) in the cache, because Windows Terminal only opens `http`, `https` and `file` links (verified 2026-10-12).
- **APP-022** [M1] **Notifications:** the suite has a Start-menu identity (AppUserModelID) used for Windows notifications. The core posts notifications through it: job finished, confirmation waiting, cap confirmation waiting, cap alerts, and director-task decisions (APP-050). A notification is drawn by Windows, not by the core, and clicking it is your own input, so the app it launches comes to the front.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-19) **Mechanism:** classic WinRT toasts (`ToastNotificationManager`) from the unpackaged suite, with no Windows App SDK runtime and no COM activator. (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: the shortcut also carries `System.AppUserModel.ToastActivatorCLSID`, registered per user, only so that a missed toast stays in the notification centre; every toast still activates by protocol, so no COM activator runs. Appendix G; `docs/spikes/11-windows-integration.md`.)
  - A per-user Start-menu shortcut carries the channel's AUMID (`System.AppUserModel.ID`, QA-038), because Windows shows no toast from an unpackaged sender without one. `lil doctor` checks the shortcut and, after you type `y`, creates or repairs it.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-22) The app offers the notification setup with one Set up button on its first start and in its settings, and writes it only after your click.
  - A click on the toast or one of its buttons activates by protocol (`activationType="protocol"`) into a `lil:` URL, such as `lil:confirm/<id>`.
  - A URI activation carries no caller identity, so it never performs an irreversible action by itself: it opens a window where a real input event decides (APP-044).
  - (owner decision 2026-10-22) A confirmation toast has Open and Deny: Deny denies from the toast, authenticated by a single-use token in its URL; approving needs the window (P4a-R9).
  - Progress toasts update in place by Tag and Group, and are replaced when the job ends (APP-146). (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: plan 4a posts no progress toasts, and (owner decision 2026-10-24) none are posted later either; APP-146. `docs/spikes/11-windows-integration.md`.)
  - Plan 4a's notification spike confirms that the core, a windowless process, can post under the shortcut's AUMID, and that the `windows` crate exposes the toast API. If either fails, the result goes to the owner before plan 4a continues. (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: the detached, windowless sender's toasts popped under the per-user shortcut's AUMID 3 times of 3, with `Setting()` `Enabled` and each toast in the notification history; with the shortcut removed and only the `AppUserModelId` registry key present, a toast also showed (S1.2). Whether a missed toast stays in the notification centre under the plain shortcut is not settled: on a Windows 11 26H2 pre-release build both shortcut forms, with and without `System.AppUserModel.ToastActivatorCLSID`, were still in the notification history 15 s after the banner, but the centre itself wasn't checked; on 25H2 a missed toast under the plain shortcut wasn't found in the centre (S1.2p). By the spike's rule the CLSID is added when a test in the notification centre itself fails or can't run, and on the test PC it can't run, since UI Automation saw no toast and no centre entry; so plan 4a's shortcut carries the CLSID from the start (Appendix G). `docs/spikes/11-windows-integration.md`.)
  - Categories, defaults, progress toasts and the fullscreen quiet mode: APP-146.
  - The shortcut's name and target, the per-user scheme and `.lilref` registration, and the toast XML with its actions, Tag and Group are fixed in Appendix G (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
- **APP-146** [M1] **Notification behaviour** (owner decision 2026-10-19):
  - **Categories,** each with its own toggle: job finished, job failed, confirmation waiting (agent safety and cap), cap alerts, director-task decisions, job progress (withdrawn 2026-10-24, below), and (owner decision 2026-10-22) Claude Code and agent activity (Claude Code's notices, and an agent's `open_in` and `ui.show`, APP-065), on by default. Defaults: progress off, every other category on. Turning off "confirmation waiting" stops its toasts only: the confirmation still waits in the Confirmations panel, and a caller with no app open then gets `confirm.unavailable` or `cap.exceeded`, since the notification path can't reach you (ARCH-055).
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23) **Notifications that scale:** the category set is open: a later plan adds a category with its key and its default. Every toast that asks something of you points at an item that stays listed in the app until it is answered, so the toast is never the only copy. FYI toasts for jobs you didn't start from your own panel are coalesced per `origin` (CORE-117), each replacing the previous one by Tag and Group (a new post, not an in-place update), and pop once, when the batch ends; toasts that ask something are never coalesced away. Confirmation records gain an optional `group: {task_id, gate_id?}`, returned by `app/confirmations.list`. The names and shapes are in Appendix G.
  - **Progress toasts** (when on): one per job expected to run longer than about 30 s while no suite window is in front, updated at most once a second. Windows never pops an update and drops one sent after the toast was dismissed, so the toast is replaced when the job ends, and the end still pops. (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: every update of the spike's progress toast returned `Succeeded`, an update after it was dismissed returned `NotificationNotFound`, and a toast posted after `Remove` popped, as documented; but a second pop was seen while it updated on Windows 11 25H2, and on a 26H2 pre-release build a toast posted with `SuppressPopup` was seen to pop, possibly the control toast posted just before (S1.5 FAIL). So plan 4a takes the FAIL path: no progress toasts. `docs/spikes/11-windows-integration.md`.) (owner decision 2026-10-24) **No live progress toasts,** in plan 4a or later: a job's progress is shown in the Jobs panel, and a toast goes out only when work finishes or something needs you. The job progress category is withdrawn, with its toggle; an old `job_progress` key in `suite.toml` is kept and ignored, like any unknown category key (Appendix G).
  - **Quiet while fullscreen** (on by default): while Windows reports a fullscreen state (`SHQueryUserNotificationState` returns `QUNS_RUNNING_D3D_FULL_SCREEN`, `QUNS_BUSY` or `QUNS_PRESENTATION_MODE`), toasts go to the notification centre silently, never dropped. Windows sends no event when this changes, so the core polls it at an interval the plan sets, in the same poll as the fullscreen auto-pause (CORE-160); it never inspects the process list. Confirmations are made silent like the rest, never suppressed.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-22) **While a suite window is in front,** no toast is posted: the app shows the same news itself (P4a-R7).
  - The categories' setting names, when each toast is posted, updated and removed, its copy, and the quiet and in-front rules are fixed in Appendix G (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
- **APP-023** [Later] Jump Lists; Explorer thumbnails for `.lildoc` (a small Rust COM thumbnail provider, out of process); an Explorer "Add to LILITH project" context-menu entry.

### 3.2 How an app talks to the core

- **APP-024** [M1] (invariant) **`lil-client` runs on a background tokio runtime inside the app; the GUI thread never waits on the core.** The stall target is in APP-135.

**Bridge (`lil-qt`, Rust via cxx-qt)**

- **APP-025** [M1] **List models** are Rust `QAbstractListModel` subclasses (cxx-qt's `#[base]`, `#[inherit]`, `#[cxx_override]`), paged through `canFetchMore`/`fetchMore`. `lil-qt` also provides the request-id API below and the document and resource models.
- **APP-026** [M1] Core events are applied on the GUI thread via `CxxQtThread::queue`; queued tasks handle the "object destroyed" error.
- **APP-027** [M1] (invariant) **Update throttling is leading-edge:** the first update applies immediately, then at most 10 Hz for job progress and 1 Hz for the telemetry samples (each sample carries the whole snapshot, so the samples are one series; plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26). Pending confirmations (agent safety and cap) are never throttled.
- **APP-028** [M1] (contract) **Requests:** one `core` singleton with `call(tool, argsJson) → requestId`, `cancel(requestId)`, and the signals `callFinished(id, result)`, `callFailed(id, error)` and `callPendingConfirmation(id, pending)` — one per result kind in §6.1 (`ok | pending_confirmation | error`). `pending` carries `confirmation_id`, `retry_token`, `expires_at` and `covers` (`confirm`, `cap` or both). A small QML helper turns these signals into callbacks/promises.
  - Per-request QObjects are not returned to QML, because QML can garbage-collect them before the request finishes.
- **APP-029** [M1] (contract) **Automatic retry after a confirmation:** when an app's own call returns `pending_confirmation` (in practice a cap, APP-054), the request stays open: the bridge (`lil-qt`'s `core` object) watches the `confirmations` topic and owns the retry, and the QML helper only decides whether to wait for it (the default) or to cancel the request. Once that confirmation is approved — inline, in the Confirmations panel or from a notification — the bridge re-sends the call once with its `retry_token` and settles the original request with what the core returns; a denial settles it with `confirm.denied`, and an expiry, or a cancel by the core, with `confirm.expired`, without a resend (§2.7, §6.1). The app never retries without an answer; if the app closes first, the pending request is cancelled. If the QML object that made the call is destroyed while the call waits on its confirmation (its panel closed), the call is detached, not cancelled: its callbacks are dropped, an approval still re-sends it once with its `retry_token`, because the approval means "do it", and its result shows wherever the core reports it (jobs, toasts); a denial or an expiry ends it as before. A call that isn't waiting on a confirmation is cancelled when that object goes (plan 4a rulings, 2026-10-26).

**C++ shim (`lil-qt-shim`)**

- **APP-030** [M1] Things cxx-qt can't do go in the C++ shim: the clipboard/drag `QMimeData` (§3.4); the live-preview image provider; docking; GPU canvases; the web view; and the notification and `lil:` activation glue.
- **APP-031** [M1] The **live-preview image provider** is a small (~50-line) `QQuickImageProvider` with a lock-protected frame map fed from Rust. Each preview frame gets a unique URL, with caching off.
- **APP-032** [M1] (invariant) For incoming clipboard and drag data, the shim only extracts the raw MIME bytes; all parsing happens in Rust.

**Connection lifecycle**

- **APP-033** [M1] **Start:** `core/hello` (the §1.2 handshake, which every caller kind uses; an app declares caller kind `ui:<app>`, protocol version and workspace) → subscribe → snapshot.
- **APP-034** [M1] **On connection loss:** a "Reconnecting…" banner; AI and workspace actions are disabled; the open document keeps working and autosaving.
- **APP-035** [M1] **On reconnect:** the app re-acquires its document holds, resubscribes from the last `seq` (or reloads snapshots if the history is gone), and saves documents changed meanwhile with a hash check against the last saved hash (conflict → keep both). It does not "save as new version".
- **APP-036** [M1] **Protocol version mismatch:** a banner offering "restart the core when it's idle".

**Images**

- **APP-037** [M1] (invariant) **Images never travel over the pipe.** Models carry file paths; QML loads images asynchronously.
- **APP-038** [M1] **Thumbnails** come from the core's thumbnail cache in the per-workspace local data folder (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\LILITH\workspaces\<hash>\thumbs\`, or `<volume>:\.lilith-local\<hash>\thumbs\` when the workspace is on a different volume from `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, §1, §7.2). Thumbnails come in fixed tiers of 96, 160, 256, 384 and 512 px × screen scale, and the GPU scales between tiers. Above 512 px, a Speed / Quality setting chooses between scaling the 512 tier and rendering a larger image on demand. The cache is keyed by the image's content SHA-256, not its path, so a rename or move (CORE-153) costs nothing, and clean-up removes the entries whose hash the index no longer holds. Grids reuse delegates and load asynchronously. (owner decision 2026-10-19: the tiers and the content key replace "at the exact display size × screen scale (two sizes on mixed-DPI setups), because only JPEG decodes cheaply at reduced size".)
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 4b) **Not image-only.** Every file has a media kind. Thumbnails, quick look, Compare (APP-141) and the inbox (CORE-179) draw each file through a small renderer registry, per kind; plan 4b ships the image renderer and a type-icon placeholder that never shows a decode error. The cache key is `(content_sha256, renderer_id@version, poster_time?)`.

### 3.3 The app interface

- **APP-039** [M1] (invariant) **`app/*` contains only what's specific to a running app:** session, subscriptions, document hold/save, activation and open requests, confirmation answers and `caps.set`, and (owner decision 2026-10-19) Pause GPU work (`app/queue.pause`, `app/queue.resume`, CORE-160), and (owner decision 2026-10-22) the desktop settings (`app/desktop.set`: the notification categories, quiet while fullscreen, fullscreen auto-pause and keep-awake; APP-146, CORE-160, ARCH-127), which only you change. Everything else is a normal `tools/*` call made with caller kind `ui:<app>`, so anything an app can do, an agent can do too (§1.1). `app/desktop.set` takes the desktop settings and `app/set_context` the session's project, and each needs its capability in the shared capabilities (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
- **APP-040** [M1] (invariant) `app/*` accepts only verified `ui:*` connections, plus a read-only subset for `cli` callers (`lil`) and tests. The handshake `core/hello` is not an `app/*` method; every caller kind calls it (§1.2).
- **APP-041** [M1] `app/*` methods run the dispatcher's resolve and validate stages (§2.2).
- **APP-042** [M1] (invariant) Document saves (`app/doc.commit`) record only index edges and events: no manifest or `progress.md` writes and no status change. The project's `links` change only when the set of inputs changes.
- **APP-043** [M1] (contract) **App interface map:**

| Area | `app/*` methods | Agent-visible tools used by apps | Event topics |
|---|---|---|---|
| Session | `app/set_context` (the handshake before it is `core/hello`, §1.2) | `projects.list`, `workspaces.list` | `session` |
| Subscriptions | `lil/subscribe`, `lil/unsubscribe` (events arrive as `lil/event`, §1.2) | — | — |
| Assets | `app/thumbs.request` | `assets.query`, `asset.get`, `asset.import` (copy or link-external), `convert.export`, `asset.signoff`, `asset.request_signoff`, `asset.mark`, `asset.rename`, `asset.move`, `asset.make_independent`, `asset.accept_move`, `asset.trash`, `asset.deprecate`, `collections.list`, `collection.save`, `collection.delete`, `trash.restore` | `project:<slug>`, `asset:<ref>` |
| Documents | `app/doc.open`, `app/doc.stage`, `app/doc.commit`, `app/doc.recover`, `app/doc.close` | `doc.list_open`, `doc.get_snapshot`, `doc.make_candidate`, and the document tools of §5.10 (`doc.layer.*` such as `doc.layer.add`, `doc.frame.set`, `doc.selection.*`, `doc.mask.from_selection`, `doc.references.*`, `doc.proposals.*`, `doc.export`) | `doc:<id>` |
| Resources | — | `resource.list`, `resource.get`, `resource.save`, `resource.delete` | `resources` |
| Jobs | `app/queue.pause`, `app/queue.resume` (CORE-160) | `jobs.list`, `job.cancel`, `job.retry`, `job.move`, `job.estimate` (the cost and time chip, APP-140) | `jobs`, `job:<id>`, `preview:<job>` |
| Confirmations (agent safety and cap) | `app/confirmations.list`, `app/confirmation.answer` | — | `confirmations` |
| Usage, caps, accounts | `app/caps.set` | `usage.summary`, `caps.get`, `accounts.list`, `accounts.refresh` | `usage`, `caps`, `accounts` |
| Local AI settings (from plan 7a) | `app/local_llm.set` (verified `ui:*` connection only, AI-228) | — | — |
| Hardware | — | `telemetry.series`, `telemetry.snapshot`, `telemetry.jobs`, `telemetry.energy` | `telemetry` |
| Notifications and desktop | `app/desktop.set` | `desktop.get` | `desktop` |
| Interchange | `app/activate` (the target app brings a window forward after the requester granted it foreground rights) | `handlers.for`, `open_in` and `ui.show` (both return `{running, pid, launch_command}`; §3.4), `lilref.write`, `notify.claude_code` (called by `lil hook notification`, not by apps) | `open_requests:<app>` |
| Diagnostics | — | `engines.status`, `diagnostics.bundle`, `models.report` (CORE-164), `diagnostics.recent`, `engines.environment` | `engines`, `diagnostics` |

`app/set_context` sets only the session's project: the app switches workspace with a new `core/hello` (ARCH-030), and `app/desktop.set` is the only way the desktop settings change (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).

**Confirmation answers**

- **APP-044** [M1] (invariant) Confirmation answers are accepted only from verified `ui:*` connections. `cli`, `mcp` and `assistant` callers can never answer, and a caller-supplied `confirm: true` is ignored (§2.7). The shared dialog sends an answer only after a real input event. The core can verify the connection, not the click; that is the guarantee, stated plainly. (owner decision 2026-10-22; widened by owner round 10, 2026-10-23) One exception, for a **deny** only: a deny of any confirmation shown in a toast, an agent's or a spending cap's, may also come from that toast's Deny action, relayed by a verified `ui:*` connection with that toast's single-use token (P4a-R9). Approving stays only in the shared dialog, after a real input event. The exception covers deny only; it never applies to `task.start` (AI-213) or to any other rule that needs a real input event.
- **APP-045** [M1] A single pending confirmation can cover an agent safety confirmation, a cap, or both (one prompt at most per call, §2.2 step 5).
- **APP-046** [M1] Every answer records how it was given (`confirmed_via`: `app` or `claude_code`). The Confirmations panel has a history tab of past answers.

**When no app is open**

- **APP-047** [M1] Destructive/exec confirmations requested by interactive Claude Code sessions are answered in Claude Code's own prompt (§2.7).
- **APP-048** [M1] Everything else — cap confirmations from Claude Code or `lil`, confirmations from `lil`, and director-task confirmations (§4.6) — uses the **notification path**: the core posts a Windows notification; clicking it opens `lil:confirm/<id>`, which launches a minimal `lil-image --confirm <id>` window in front. Director-task decisions (`waiting_quota`, `waiting_auth`) also start with a notification but are made in the Assistant panel (APP-050). The core never shows a dialog of its own.
- **APP-049** [M1] `lil --confirm-window` launches the same minimal window directly. It's opt-in, for interactive use, not for scripts. It is spelled `lil confirm-window [<id>]`: it starts the channel's `lil-image.exe` with `--confirm [<id>]`, the window lists every pending confirmation when no id is given, and with `--non-interactive` the command refuses (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
- **APP-050** [M1] **Director-task decisions** — the three `waiting_quota` choices and the `waiting_auth` choices (§4.6a) — are shown in the **Assistant panel**, not in the minimal confirmation window:
  - the core posts a notification naming the task, the Claude Code profile (by its label) and, for `waiting_quota`, the reset time; clicking it opens `lil:task/<job_id>`, which launches or brings forward the Image studio with the Assistant panel on that task;
  - the panel lists the choices: for `waiting_quota` — wait for the reset (the default; the reset time is shown), switch to the API engine, and use a local model (M1.5, shown disabled in M1); for `waiting_auth` — sign in again (the app opens a terminal for `/login` in that profile; the core never launches it), use a different profile, and switch to the API engine;
  - the Jobs panel shows the substatus with an "Open in Assistant" action. If you don't answer, a `waiting_quota` task takes the default (it waits and resumes at the reset time) and a `waiting_auth` task keeps waiting; the suite never picks a profile for you (§4.6a).
- **APP-051** [M1] (contract) A call returns `pending_confirmation` whenever the confirmation can be answered (an app is connected or the notification path can reach you). Callers that declare themselves non-interactive (`lil --non-interactive`, scheduled tasks) never get `pending_confirmation`: a cap returns the error `cap.exceeded`, and an agent safety confirmation returns the error `confirm.unavailable` (§2.7, §6.1). (plan 3 clarification: Appendix F) In plan 3 a confirmation can be answered only while a verified `ui:*` connection is open; the notification path is plan 4 and reports unreachable until then. From plan 4a the notification path is built, and it can reach you when the channel's toasts are ready, its AUMID shortcut and handlers are in place and "confirmation waiting" is on (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).

**Assistant calls and app lifetime**

- **APP-052** [M1] (invariant) **TETHER** calls come from an open app; if that app closes mid-turn, its pending calls are cancelled.
- **APP-053** [M1] (invariant) **Director tasks** are jobs (`kind: assistant_task`, §4.6) owned by the core: they keep running when the app closes, and their pending confirmations stay pending and use the notification path (APP-048).

**Cap confirmations on your own actions**

- **APP-054** [M1] A cap confirmation on your own action appears inline at the point of action (raise the cap / allow once / cancel) and also in the Confirmations panel. Answering it anywhere clears it everywhere.
- **APP-140** [M1] **Cost and time chip** (owner decision 2026-10-20; plan 4a, then each panel's plan). Every action that spends GPU time or money shows the estimate before you click, from `job.estimate` (CORE-161): for example "local · ~8 s · ~2 Wh" or "≈ 0.04 USD · 3 % of cap". With no history it says "no estimate yet". (owner decision 2026-10-21) An included route shows its simulated cost, labelled as such (CORE-171). No included route exists before plans 6b and 7+, so plan 4a builds only the real-estimate chip. The chip informs only; caps and confirmations gate as before (APP-054). The Generate panel's chip comes with plan 6a.

### 3.4 Interchange between apps

**References**

- **APP-055** [M1] (contract) **Asset references** everywhere use `lil://<workspace>/<project>/<asset>@<version>` (§1.2). References to a sealed version — `lil://` refs, `.lilref` files, exports and engine-import records — also carry that version's SHA-256 content hash (§2.3, CORE-047).
- **APP-056** [M1.5] When multi-machine sync has renumbered a sealed version, references resolve through its `renamed_from` record using the content hash they carry, with a warning on a mismatch (§7.3).
- **APP-057** [M1] (contract) **Candidates** are referenced as `@candidate/<run>/<file>`, for viewing and provenance only. **Proposals** are referenced as `doc:<doc_id>/proposal/<run_id>/<n>`, for viewing and recalling settings only (§5.5).

**Formats**

- **APP-058** [M1] (decision) **One native layered format for every 2D app: the LILITH document** (§3.5). Flattened files are for leaving the suite.
- **APP-059** [M1b] **ORA import** is best effort (TIFF and ORA import/export arrive together, §2.10).
- **APP-060** [M1b] **ORA export** is lossy and shows a warning listing what's lost: masks are baked into alpha, transforms are rasterised, blend modes outside ORA's 20 are approximated, and float depth is reduced to 16-bit (OpenRaster spec, verified 2026-10-12).

**App handler registry**

- **APP-061** [M1] (contract) Each installed app ships `apps/<app>.toml` declaring the asset types and formats it can open, edit and create, plus its "send to" actions. External programs register the same way. `handlers.for` answers "what can open this?".
- **APP-062** [Later] "Edit in external app" (your default program or a chosen exe; flow in APP-110) and bridges to Krita, Blender and Godot.
- **APP-162** [Later] (decision) **Game-engine export** (owner decisions 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21; from the 2D studio on).
  - Godot and Unity are both export targets from the 2D studio on, built together.
  - Export goes to a bundle folder by default. An opt-in **sync to project** writes into the engine project itself, and only while that project's editor is closed.
  - Two facts are unverified, and a spike at the start of the 2D export work settles them before any exporter is built: whether Godot and Unity accept import sidecars the suite writes by hand (Godot's `.import` files, Unity's `.meta` files), and how the suite can tell reliably that a project's editor is open.
  - Unity is installed when the 2D export work starts. Until it is installed, Unity exports are labelled "unverified".

**Open in / send to**

- **APP-063** [M1] (contract) The requester (an app, `lil`, or `lil-mcp` on behalf of an agent) calls `open_in(app, target, intent)` or `ui.show(target)`; the core returns `{running, pid, launch_command}`.
  - If the target app is running, the requester calls `AllowSetForegroundWindow(pid)` (this works when the requester itself has foreground rights, e.g. the app you're using); then the core delivers `open_requests:<app>` and the target activates (`app/activate`).
  - If it isn't running, the requester launches it with the request on its command line. `lil-mcp` may launch apps on an agent's behalf, without focus (below). The launch command's arguments are the registered `args`, then `--intent <intent>`, then the target as a base64url `open/` URL (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
- **APP-064** [M1] (invariant) **The core never launches GUI apps.** It may sit in a terminal's job object, and Windows wouldn't let its windows come forward.
- **APP-065** [M1] (invariant) **Agents never steal focus.** `ui.show` and agent `open_in` show the content without activating the window (taskbar flash) and post a notification; clicking it brings the window forward. When Claude Code runs in a focused terminal, a launched app may come forward on its own; that's a bonus, not relied on. From plan 4a that notification is an `agent_activity` toast, which opens the target when clicked (plan 4a clarification: Appendix G).
- **APP-066** [M1] (contract) **Intents:** `open`; `edit` (creates or resumes a working document); `use_as_input`.
- **APP-067** [Later] Cross-app send-to beyond the Image studio (arrives with the second app).

**Clipboard and drag-out** (one `QMimeData` subclass in the C++ shim)

- **APP-068** [M1] (contract) Copy and drag-out offer these formats:
  - `application/x-lilith-ref` (refs plus type, version and thumbnail path) for the suite;
  - a standard bitmap plus the exact PNG bytes (Windows "PNG" format) for image-aware programs;
  - a file URL to an exported copy for Explorer, browsers and Discord;
  - no plain text by default (it makes some programs paste text instead of the image). "Copy reference" is a separate command.

  *Recipe note* (M0 result, 2026-10-12: the recipe in `docs/spikes/07-clipboard.md` §3.1 is confirmed. The exact PNG bytes need `setData("application/x-qt-windows-mime;value=\"PNG\"", bytes)`, because a plain `image/png` is re-encoded by Qt; the bitmap comes from `setImageData`, the file URL from `setUrls` (CF_HDROP); no `setText`. Qt also adds `UniformResourceLocator(W)` and 17 `image/*` formats that can't be removed through `QMimeData`; if an app ever pastes a link instead of the image, the fallback is a native `IDataObject`.)
- **APP-069** [M1] (invariant) **Drags allow Copy only.** Qt's default would let Explorer *move* the file. (M0 result, 2026-10-12: `QDrag::exec(Qt::CopyAction, Qt::CopyAction)` confirmed; the manual check reported that an Explorer drag copied the file. `docs/spikes/07-clipboard.md`.)
- **APP-070** [M1] (invariant) **Exported copies** live in a cache, aren't read-only, are never workspace paths, and are produced lazily when a program asks for them. The cache never evicts a file that's still on the clipboard or in a drag.
- **APP-071** [M0] Pasting into Discord and Chromium is tested in the M0 spike (and again in the manual milestone checks, §6.4). (M0 result, 2026-10-12: the manual check reported that all checks (Discord, Chrome, Paint, Explorer drag) worked; only Chrome was named. `docs/spikes/07-clipboard.md`.)

**Drag-in and paste**

- **APP-072** [M1] Files from Explorer arrive as file URLs; web images arrive as http(s) URLs and are downloaded by the core. Virtual files (Outlook attachments, zip folders) come later.
- **APP-073** [M1] Dropped or pasted content goes into the current document as a layer or an input, or becomes new assets via `asset.import`: copy, or "link external" for big loose files (path + hash recorded, checked on open). The source (file path, URL or app) is recorded as provenance.
- **APP-074** [M1] (invariant) **Link-external assets** can be viewed and used as layer pixels, but can't be signed off or live-linked until "Collect into workspace" copies them in. Sealed versions must be inside the workspace and read-only.

**Live links**

- **APP-075** [M1] Documents that use another asset as an input store its ref in `doc.json`. The index turns these into graph edges on every save, marked `local` (documents are machine-local, §3.5), so "what uses this?", staleness and cascades include working documents even when they're closed.
- **APP-076** [M1] A new sealed version of a linked input shows "update available"; pinning keeps the old one.
- **APP-077** [M1] (invariant) **Only sealed versions can be live-linked.** Dropping a candidate (or a proposal) embeds its pixels and records `derived_from_candidate` as provenance. That record sits outside the recipe inputs (which must be sealed) and may dangle after the 90-day candidate purge, which is accepted. The drop UI offers "sign off first".

**Libraries (shared resources)**

- **APP-078** [M1] (contract) Libraries are typed resources usable in every app and by agents. Workspace resources live in `library/resources/<kind>/`; personal resources live in `%APPDATA%\LILITH\resources\` and are available in no-workspace mode. One shared Libraries panel; changes are published on the `resources` topic.
- **APP-079** [M1a] Resource kinds in M1a: `swatch`, `palette`, `prompt_preset`, `style_preset` (prompt + LoRAs + settings for a compatible model-profile family, §5.6), `model_profile` (§5.6; personal by default, since models are machine-level, with workspace overrides), and `prompt_brief` (a promoted Prompt Studio brief, AI-183). (owner decision 2026-10-19) The `wildcard` kind follows in M1b (IMG-162).
- **APP-080** [M1a] Resource kind `workflow`: a ComfyUI workflow with marked slots (§5.6, §5.7). It's saved from the ComfyUI tab (M1a) and opened by the Graph tab and workflow-on-layer (M1b).
- **APP-081** [Later] Resource kinds `gradient`, `export_preset`, `brush` and `text_style` (the last two with the matching Image studio features, §5.11).
- **APP-082** [M1] (invariant) `resource.delete` is soft (to trash) and is `destructive`, so it needs confirmation when an agent asks.

### 3.5 Documents (things you edit by hand)

**The LILITH document** is the suite's native editable format. §5.2 defines the layer model and chunk encoding.

- **APP-083** [M1] (contract) **Inside a workspace**, a document is a folder `assets/<id>/work/<doc_id>/` containing:
  - `doc.json` — layer tree, blend modes, masks, transforms, linked inputs (refs), and `based_on` (the version or candidate it started from). (owner decision 2026-10-20) Transforms are kept as parameters over unchanged chunks until you rasterise them (IMG-056), and saved selections are named masks (IMG-022);
  - `chunks/` — layer data as **content-addressed chunks** (1024×1024-px chunks of 8×8 tiles on a signed grid, each with a tile-presence/uniform header bitmap; §5.2). Each chunk is one file, `chunks/<sha256>.chunk`, where `<sha256>` is the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the chunk's canonical uncompressed bytes; the file holds the header and the zstd-compressed data. Raster and mask chunks share the folder. `doc.json` maps each layer's signed chunk coordinates `(cx, cy)` to a chunk hash, so a save writes only chunks that don't exist yet;
  - `thumbnail.png`;
  - `proposals/` — the document's proposal runs (APP-095).
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 5) **Forward compatibility, with room reserved.** `doc.json` carries `format_version` and a `features` list. A build that meets a newer version or an unknown feature opens the document read-only ("made by a newer LILITH") and never sweeps its chunks (APP-093). An unknown layer kind round-trips losslessly and is shown read-only, never composited, as AI-164 treats unknown brief fields. `doc.json` names its document kind; `image` is the only value plan 5 writes (APP-097). Reserved, with plan 5 accepting only today's value: a chunk element-format code (IMG-023); a `timeline` (a frame axis and a per-frame cel key); `wrap: none | x | y | xy`; `colour_role: colour | data`, per layer (plan 5 writes `data` only for an image imported as data, IMG-169); a palette reference, as a linked input (APP-075), for the reserved indexed mode; and a `grid` block.
- **APP-084** [M1] (invariant) Chunk files are immutable: they're written once under their content hash and never modified in place (snapshots hardlink them).
- **APP-085** [M1] (contract) **Outside a workspace** (loose files, no-workspace mode), the same layout (`doc.json`, `chunks/`, `thumbnail.png`, `proposals/`) is stored as a single uncompressed-zip file, **`.lildoc`**, which is what file associations and Explorer thumbnails use. "Add to project" unpacks a `.lildoc` into a workspace folder, and its open proposals move with it.

**Three kinds of "staging"** — these names are used consistently across the spec:
- **store staging handles** — temporary areas `store` issues to engines and converters (`.lilith/staging/`, §2.0);
- **document save handles** — what `app/doc.stage` returns for one document save;
- **proposals** — generation results waiting on the canvas's staging bar (§5.5).

**Saving**

- **APP-086** [M1] (contract) **Save sequence:** the app asks `store` for a document save handle (`app/doc.stage`), writes the changed chunks and `doc.json` there, then calls `app/doc.commit(handle, expected_hash)`. `store` checks the hash (conflict → keep both; error `store.hash_conflict`), moves the files in atomically, records the new hash, and tells the watcher to ignore its own write.
- **APP-087** [M1] (invariant) **Opening, editing and saving never change the asset's status.** A save updates index edges and emits events only — no manifest, `progress.md` or status change. The asset gets a `has_working_doc` flag so Home and the Asset browser can show "being edited". `auto_candidate_on_save` (APP-101) is the only kind of save that changes status, and it does so through a candidate run.
- **APP-088** [M1] (invariant) **Documents are machine-local:** `work/` is gitignored (`lil doctor` adds `assets/*/work/` to `.gitignore`). On another clone of the workspace, documents simply aren't there until restored from a checkpoint (APP-092).

**Protecting work**

- **APP-089** [M1] **Autosave** every 30 s to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\LILITH\recovery\<app>\`; after a crash, `app/doc.recover` offers the recovery file.
- **APP-090** [M1] **Rolling snapshots:** the last 5 saves plus one per day for 14 days, kept in a separate content-addressed snapshot store in the per-workspace local data folder, which is always on the workspace's volume: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\LILITH\workspaces\<hash>\snapshots\`, or `<volume>:\.lilith-local\<hash>\snapshots\` when the workspace is on a different volume from `%LOCALAPPDATA%` (§1, §7.2). The store holds `chunks/<sha256>.chunk` (shared by all snapshots) and one `doc.json` per snapshot under `<doc_id>/`. Chunks are hardlinked from the document (deduplicated at chunk level) and copied only when the file system can't hardlink (e.g. an exFAT drive). Snapshots survive even if `work/` is wiped (e.g. by `git clean -X`). (owner decision 2026-10-20; plan 5) A named snapshot made with "Snapshot now" (IMG-042) is kept as a rolling-snapshot entry in the same store, so it survives restarts, and carries its name in the History panel.
- **APP-091** [M1] **Backups:** `work/`, proposals and the rolling snapshots are included in the suite's restic backups (§7.4). Diagnostics shows the backup status line (APP-134).
- **APP-092** [M1.5] **Checkpoints:** an explicit "Checkpoint document" uploads the document's content-addressed chunks and `doc.json` to the `docs/` prefix of the S3-compatible large-file store (§7.2); unchanged chunks are never re-uploaded. "Restore documents from checkpoint" brings documents to another machine. Retention is a suite-side mark-and-sweep that uses a separate delete-capable key reserved for that job (the suite asks before its first run). Without a cloud store, checkpoints aren't available; local snapshots still work.

**Garbage collection**

- **APP-093** [M1] (invariant) `store` runs a mark-and-sweep over a document's chunks while holding the document. Roots: the current `doc.json`, every snapshot's `doc.json`, open document save handles, and copies in progress. New chunks get a 24 h grace period.
  - (owner decision 2026-10-23, round 13; reservation for plan 5) GC, rolling snapshots (APP-090) and Make a take (APP-099) share **one chunk-reference enumerator**, which marks every chunk hash anywhere in `doc.json`, in fields this build doesn't know too. A document made by a newer build (APP-083) is never swept.
- **APP-094** [M1] (invariant) Proposal runs are not GC roots: accepting a proposal copies its pixels into new layer chunks. Sealed and candidate `source/` folders are independent copies and never part of a document's GC. (owner decision 2026-10-19) A layer's proposal origin (IMG-083) keeps its run on disk longer (IMG-082), but it is not a GC root: the layer's pixels are its own chunks.

**Proposals**

- **APP-095** [M1] (invariant) **Proposal runs** live in `work/<doc_id>/proposals/<run_id>/` (or the app cache for unsaved and loose documents) and are separate from asset candidate runs: they create no asset versions, change no status and don't run the QA chain. (owner decision 2026-10-21) It does get the checks of CORE-172 before it is shown. They're kept until accepted or discarded, then for 14 days, and they move with a `.lildoc` on "Add to project". Anchoring, the staging bar and destinations are in §5.5. (owner decision 2026-10-19) A run that a layer records as its origin is kept longer (IMG-082).

**New and existing documents**

- **APP-096** [M1] **New documents** are unsaved (recovery file only) until the first Save, which opens "Save to project" (default `sketchbook`) and creates the asset with status `in_progress`. In no-workspace mode the first Save writes a `.lildoc`.
- **APP-097** [M1] **Which document opens:** one primary document per asset per kind; others are listed and can be renamed or discarded (discard = trash).
- **APP-098** [M1] Editing a candidate seeds a new document from that candidate; editing an approved asset seeds one from its sealed `source/` (APP-100).

**From document to version**

- **APP-099** [M1] **"Make a take"** (and "Save & make a take", Ctrl+Shift+S) renders the document into a candidate run (role `manual`) **and copies the document at that exact state** (`doc.json` plus the chunks it uses) into `candidates/<run_id>/source/`. The recipe records the document hash and its inputs resolved to sealed versions.
- **APP-100** [M1] (invariant) **Sign-off moves the chosen file and its `source/`** into the sealed version (§2.3's move rule), so any sealed version can be reopened for editing exactly as it was. `source/` is not a companion: it's excluded from `id@version` refs and from exports.
- **APP-101** [M1] `auto_candidate_on_save` is a project option, off by default, that makes every save also run "Make a take".
