  - CPU package power comes from the Energy Meter counter set, whose energy values are picowatt-hours: the raw value is accumulated as an integer and converted to mJ (× 36 / 10⁷) only when read; without the counter set, CPU utilisation × `[power] cpu_tdp_w`, labelled `estimated`; with neither, the CPU part is `unknown`, shown as missing and left out of the whole-system and per-job CPU figures (never guessed);
  - whole system = (GPU + CPU package + `rest_of_system_w`) / `psu_efficiency`, each part labelled `measured`, `estimated` or `unknown`; `suite.toml` `[power]`: `psu_efficiency = "0.9"`, `rest_of_system_w = "0"` (0 means "not set" and is shown so), optional `cpu_tdp_w`, optional `electricity = {rate, currency}` per kWh;
  - alerts on the `telemetry` topic: GPU temperature ≥ 85 °C, and thermal or power-brake throttle reasons; each raised once and cleared once;
  - per-job attribution: a job's ComfyUI execution windows, each with the engine's processes when it closed (never pid 0, which is every untracked process; a window whose processes are unknown is not reported); GPU energy split by the engine process's share of SM utilisation, CPU energy by its share of CPU time; gross and above idle, where idle is the median GPU power of the 60 s before the window with utilisation under 5 %, else the lowest 1-minute average of the last hour; GPU-seconds are SM-weighted;
  - the hardware profile `{gpu, vram_gb, ram_gb, detected}` is written to the workspace file's `hardware` key (a targeted YAML edit) only when `gpu`, `vram_gb` or `ram_gb` differs;
  - CPU temperature is never sampled (CORE-060).
- **Confirmations** (ARCH-052–055, CORE-109–115, APP-044–046):
  - a confirmation lives in the `state.db` of its call's scope, keyed by `(idempotency scope, key)`, bound to the tool and the arguments' hash, and expires after **30 minutes** (owner question 6);
  - one confirmation covers `confirm`, `cap` or both, and `covers` lists `confirm` before `cap` (`["confirm", "cap"]`); states `pending`, `approved`, `denied`, `expired`, `cancelled`; `confirmed_via` is `app` or `claude_code`;
  - a connection is `mcp` only when `lil-mcp.exe` runs from a trusted folder and its direct parent process is `claude.exe` (no shell in between) and started before it, so a process that took an exited parent's pid does not count; otherwise it is `cli`. The parent is recognised by its file name, so a program renamed `claude.exe` passes: `mcp_confirmation` stays `"core"` until a stronger parent check exists;
  - `mcp_confirmation` is recorded here as **`"core"`** (verified 2026-10-18, Appendix B.3). It decides how an `mcp` connection's `destructive` and `exec` calls are confirmed: `"core"` (the default) treats an `mcp` call like any agent call, so it gets `pending_confirmation` while an app is connected and `confirm.unavailable` otherwise, and the core does **not** mark those tools with `_meta["anthropic/requiresUserInteraction"]` in `tools/list` for `mcp` connections, so the core's confirmation is the only prompt (no double prompts); `"claude_code"`, allowed only if Claude Code honours the marker in every permission mode (including `bypassPermissions` and auto mode), marks them with `_meta["anthropic/requiresUserInteraction"]: true` in `tools/list`, lets the call proceed and records it `approved` with `confirmed_via: claude_code`. `assistant` connections (plan 7+) aren't marked;
  - a call that needs a confirmation returns `pending_confirmation` while a verified `ui:*` connection is open (plan 4 adds the notification path); otherwise, and always for a non-interactive caller, it returns `confirm.unavailable` or `cap.exceeded`;
  - a `ui:*` session's pending cap confirmations are cancelled when its connection closes (from plan 4a: when its app's process exits, Appendix G "Connections");
  - `retry_token` is the call's idempotency key. A retry with that key re-evaluates the stored `pending_confirmation`: approved runs the call (once; its outcome is then stored as usual), denied returns `confirm.denied`, expired returns `confirm.expired` (both release the key), and still pending returns the same `pending_confirmation`.
- **Usage records** (CORE-069, CORE-071, workspace format I10): one line per call in `registry/usage.<machine>.<yyyy-mm>.jsonl`, or `usage-loose.jsonl` without a workspace, after `machine` and `seq`: `ts, workspace` (id or `"loose"`), `project, asset, job_id, tool, caller_kind, account` (opaque id), `provider, model, route, service_tier, region, billing_basis` (`payg`, `prepaid_credits`, `subscription_quota`, `free_tier`, `byok`, `local`), the units that apply (`input_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens`, `cache_ttl`, `output_tokens`, `reasoning_tokens`, `images`, `seconds`, `characters`, `requests`, `gpu_seconds`, `energy_wh_gross`, `energy_wh_above_idle`), `idle_unknown?` (as in `JobEnergy`), `raw_usage, est_cost, cost, energy_cost, cost_source, price_version, currency, latency_ms, status` (`pending`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `cancelled`), `attribution` (`suite` or `unattributed`). Money fields are decimal strings in the shortest exact form, with no trailing zeros and no trailing point (`"2"`, `"0.9"`, `"0"`). A `pending` row is written to the scope's `state.db` before the call is sent; a row left by a dead core becomes a `status: "pending"` line at startup and keeps its reservation. A paid outside prompt's `outside_paid` row is its write-ahead record instead (ruling P3-R27): it gets no `pending` row, and no second line when this machine's log already has a `comfyui.outside_prompt` line for its prompt id (`raw_usage.prompt_id`). A job that reported execution windows gets one local-work line (`billing_basis: "local"`, `cost: "0"`, its energy, and `energy_cost` at `[power] electricity` when set), unless its handler recorded its own usage draft (the node install), which is then its only line; an `interrupted_unknown` engine job you resolve gets a `status: "cancelled"` local-work line, with its energy when this core run saw its windows, else without (they ended with the run that left it unknown). In a usage record `gpu_seconds`, `energy_wh_gross` and `energy_wh_above_idle` are decimal strings (Wh with at most 6 decimals, a 3.6 mJ resolution, rounded half to even): gross is the job's whole-system energy (`JobEnergy.system_mj`), above idle is its GPU and CPU energy above idle over `psu_efficiency` (the rest-of-system baseline is idle by definition), and `energy_cost` is the whole-system energy at `[power] electricity`, in that rate's `currency`.
- **Caps and money in `suite.toml`** (CORE-081, CORE-083, CORE-085–092):
  - `[money] display_currency = "USD"`; `[[money.rates]] currency, in_display, date` (1 unit of `currency` = `in_display` display units);
  - `[caps]` `monthly`, `per_call_max`, `[caps.provider_monthly]`: decimal strings or integers in the display currency; the workspace format's `monthly_usd`, `per_call_max_usd` and `[caps.provider_monthly_usd]` are read only when the display currency is `USD` and the new key is absent; `app/caps.set` writes the new keys and removes the legacy ones, keeping comments;
  - `[[fixed_costs]] name, amount, currency, period = "monthly", account?`;
  - the period is the local calendar month (`YYYY-MM`, CORE-012); reservations and settled totals live in the suite `state.db`; a reservation records its exchange rate, workspace (or none) and job or call id; settling is idempotent by reservation id; an `interrupted_unknown` job's reservation stays held at the reserved amount, and resolving it with `job.cancel` settles it at that amount (ruling P3-R15); caps count only `payg`, `prepaid_credits` and `byok` money.
  - every call reserves its counted estimate whether caps are set or not (CORE-086), so a cap set mid-month counts the month's spend; a negative estimate is `caps.invalid`; with a cap that applies, an estimate in a currency with no rate is `caps.invalid`, and with none it is reserved in its own currency;
  - a reservation counts in the local month it was made in, and its settle adds to that month, so `caps.get` and `usage.summary` (whose line carries the settle time) can disagree across a month end; totals kept in another currency (a former display currency, or a reservation made without a rate) are converted at the newest rate dated on or before today, and without one the cap counts as exceeded;
  - approving a confirmation that covers `cap` needs `cap.action`, else `params.invalid`: `allow_once` lets that one call pass the caps the prompt named, up to the estimate it showed, in the display currency and at the exchange rate it showed (a cap it didn't name, a larger estimate, or another currency or rate asks again under the same retry token); `raise` first sets `raise_to {cap, amount}` with `app/caps.set`'s rules; deny is the cancel;
  - a `caps` that isn't a table, or a `suite.toml` that can't be parsed or read, fails closed: `monthly` and `per_call_max` count as invalid;
  - known limits (fail closed): an `interrupted` job with external side effects whose completions never ran keeps its reservation held for its month; a dead core's reservations in a workspace other than the default are repaired once that workspace opens as the default; when a scope's cap repair fails, the meter's recovery of that scope is skipped, so its dead core's usage rows and held reservations wait for a later start, and the meter never removes a call's usage row while its reservation is held and undecided (ruling P3-R33); a call's reservation whose hand-over to its job failed is decided by that job.
- **Resources** (APP-078–080, APP-082): `library/resources/<kind>/<id>.json` in the workspace, `%APPDATA%\LILITH\resources\<kind>\<id>.json` for personal ones; `{schema_version: 1, kind, id, name, created, updated, data}`; kinds `swatch`, `palette`, `prompt_preset`, `style_preset`, `model_profile`, `prompt_brief`, `workflow`; ids match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,62}$` and are not a Windows device name (`con`, `prn`, `aux`, `nul`, `com1`–`com9`, `lpt1`–`lpt9`; `params.invalid`, CORE-040); a `name` has at most 200 characters and no control characters, and a resource file is at most 1 MiB (`params.invalid`); `scope` defaults to `workspace` when the session has one, else `personal`, and `model_profile` defaults to `personal`; `resource.save` over an existing resource first moves the old file to the matching trash (reason `"resource.save"`), so `trash.restore` can bring it back; the `path` that `resource.save` and `resource.get` return is relative to the scope's library root (`<workspace>\library\resources\` or `%APPDATA%\LILITH\resources\`), with forward slashes: `<kind>/<id>.json`. `resource.get`, `resource.delete` and `resource.list` without a `scope` look in every scope the session can see; a `get` or `delete` of an id that exists in both scopes is refused with `resource.ambiguous_scope`, and every `get`, `delete` and `list` result names its `scope`. `trash.list` and `trash.restore` take `scope` (`workspace` or `personal`): without it `trash.list` lists every trash the session can see, each row with its `scope`, and `trash.restore` tries the workspace trash and then the personal trash, `%APPDATA%\LILITH\resources\.trash\`, which has the workspace trash's record shape and 30-day purge; restoring a resource file publishes `saved` with `restored_entry_id`. An unscoped `trash.list` that can't read one of the trashes (a link in its path, say) still lists the other and names the refused one in the result's `warnings`; it fails only when neither can be read. A trash folder that is a link (junction or symbolic link) is never used, listed, restored from or purged, in either trash (CORE-110).
- **Interchange** (APP-061–066): app registrations are `apps\<app>.toml` beside the core executable, plus `%APPDATA%\LILITH\apps\<name>.toml` for external programs: `{app, exe, args?, opens: [{types, formats, intents}], send_to?}`; `open_in` and `ui.show` return `{running, pid, launch_command: {exe, args} | null}` and, when the app is running, publish `{target, intent, focus}` on `open_requests:<app>`; `focus` is `false` for agent callers; the core never launches an app. A `.lilref` file is `{"lilref": 1, "ref": "lil://…@<v>#sha256=<hex>", "title"?}`.
- **Holds** (APP-103–105): in the core's memory, tied to the connection; a marker row in the workspace `state.db` is cleared at startup; the core takes its own hold for each operation on a closed document; a write from a non-holder fails with `doc.held_elsewhere`.
- **Product tools.** Inputs reject unknown fields.

  | Tool | Effect | Module | Arguments | `structured` result |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `jobs.list` | read | `jobs` | `{status?, kind?, project?, ids?, limit? (1–500, default 100), cursor?}` | `{jobs: [JobSummary], next_cursor}` (newest first) |
  | `job.wait` | read | `jobs` | `{job_id, timeout_s? (1–90, default 90)}` | `{job, done}`; `done` is true once the job is final or `interrupted_unknown` (only you can resolve that) |
  | `job.cancel` | write | `jobs` | `{job_id}` | `{job}`; a finished job is returned as it is |
  | `job.retry` | write | `jobs` | `{job_id}`, or from plan 4a `{batch_id}` (Appendix G) | `{job, retry_of}`, or for `{batch_id}` `{retried: [{job, retry_of}], refused: [{job_id, error}]}`; only `failed` or `cancelled`, never `interrupted_unknown`; from plan 4a only the newest attempt, never a job already retried, and a retried job keeps its `batch_id` and `index` |
  | `engines.status` | read | `engines` | `{}` | `{engines: [{engine, class, state, url, pid?, version?, listen, outside_jobs: {running, queued}, suite_job?, restart_needed, inventory?: {models, refreshed}}]}` |
  | `engines.start` | write | `engines` | `{engine: "comfyui"}` | `{engine}` |
  | `engines.stop`, `engines.restart` | destructive | `engines` | `{engine: "comfyui"}` | `{engine}`; `engine.attached` for an attached engine |
  | `engines.comfyui.install_node` | exec | `engines` | `{id, version}` | `{job}` |
  | `usage.summary` | read | `meter` | `{from?, to?, period: "day" \| "month", group_by?: [provider \| model \| project \| workspace \| caller_kind \| tool \| account]}` | `{bases: {money, subscription_quota, local}, by_currency, display_total, fixed_costs, unattributed, rows}`: each basis is an object with at least `records: n` (the period's usage records of that basis); the money and subscription-quota bases, unattributed spend and their rows also carry `units: {"<unit>": n}`, the record units they add up (tokens, images, seconds, ...; local work carries GPU-seconds and energy as fields of its own); `by_currency: {"<ISO 4217>": "<decimal>"}`, money basis only; `display_total: "<decimal>"` in the display currency: the money records at their rates, plus the period's fixed costs when `period: "month"`; `fixed_costs: [{name, amount, currency, display_amount}]`; the records read are `usage-loose.jsonl` and, when the calling session has a workspace, that workspace's usage logs; a record settled at its estimate (`cost_source: "estimated"`) or without a cost counts as estimated; a sum that would pass the micro-unit range leaves that addend out and the result says `overflow: true` |
  | `caps.get` | read | `meter` | `{}` | `CapsView {display_currency, caps: [{cap, limit, settled, held, ratio_permille, invalid?}], period}` |
  | `telemetry.snapshot` | read | `telemetry` | `{}` | `Snapshot` |
  | `telemetry.series` | read | `telemetry` | `{metrics, from, to, resolution?: "1s" \| "1m" \| "1h"}` | `{series: [{metric, points: [[ts, min, avg, max]]}]}`; more than 3,600 points per metric is `params.invalid` |
  | `telemetry.jobs` | read | `telemetry` | `{job_ids?, from?, to?, limit?}` | `{jobs: [JobEnergy]}`, newest first: the jobs of every workspace and loose ones, whatever the session's workspace |
  | `handlers.for` | read | `interchange` | `{target}` | `{handlers: [{app, intents, external}]}` |
  | `open_in` | read | `interchange` | `{app, target, intent: "open" \| "edit" \| "use_as_input"}` | `{running, pid, launch_command}` |
  | `ui.show` | read | `interchange` | `{target}` | as `open_in` |
  | `resource.save` | write | `resources` | `{kind, id, name, data, scope?}` | `{resource, path, replaced_entry_id?}` (the trash entry of the version it replaced) |
  | `resource.list` | read | `resources` | `{kind?, scope?}` | `{resources: [{kind, id, name, scope, updated}]}` |
  | `resource.get` | read | `resources` | `{kind, id, scope?}` | `{resource, path, scope}` |
  | `resource.delete` | destructive | `resources` | `{kind, id, scope?}` | `{entry_id, scope}` |
  | `doc.list_open` | read | `docs` | `{}` | `{documents: [{doc_id, path, held_by}]}` |

- **`app/*` methods** (APP-043; verified `ui:*` only, except the read-only `app/confirmations.list`, which `cli` may call too):

  | Method | Params | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | `app/confirmations.list` | `{state: "pending" \| "history", limit? (1–500, default 100)}` | `{confirmations: [{id, tool, arguments (untrusted), caller_kind, covers, cap?, state, confirmed_via?, created, expires_at, answered?, group?}]}`; `group` (`{task_id, gate_id?}`) from plan 4a, set only by the director plan (Appendix G) |
  | `app/confirmation.answer` | `{confirmation_id, answer: "approve" \| "deny", cap?: {action: "allow_once" \| "raise", raise_to?: {cap, amount}}, toast_token?}`; `toast_token` from plan 4a, with `answer: "deny"` only (Appendix G, "Deny from a toast") | `{confirmation}` |
  | `app/caps.set` | `{monthly?, per_call_max?, provider_monthly?: {<provider>: amount \| null}}` (`null` clears) | `CapsView` |

- **Topics:** `jobs` (`{kind: "job", job}`); `job:<id>` (`progress`, `status`, `retrying`, `watchdog`; snapshot the `JobSummary`); `preview:<job_id>`; `engines` (`status`, `outside_job`, `restart_needed`); `confirmations` (`pending`, `answered`, `expired`, `cancelled`); `usage` (`record`); `caps` (`alert` `{kind: "alert", cap, threshold_pct: 50 or 80 or 100, spent_pct, spent, currency, period: "YYYY-MM"}`, where (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough) `spent_pct` is the settled total as a whole percentage of the cap, rounded down, when the alert was raised, and (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough review) `spent` that total as a decimal string in the display currency `currency`; for a cap of 0, which has no percentage, `spent_pct` is `null`, `changed`, `invalid`); `telemetry` (`sample`, `alert`, `alert_cleared`; snapshot the `Snapshot`); `resources` (`saved` `{kind: "saved", resource_kind, id, scope, updated, replaced_entry_id?}`, or `{kind: "saved", resource_kind, id, scope, restored_entry_id}` for a restored resource; `deleted` `{kind: "deleted", resource_kind, id, scope, entry_id}`); `open_requests:<app>`.
- **MCP lowering** (ARCH-012, ARCH-039, AI-110–113, QA-002, QA-010): server name `lil`; `instructions` of at most 1,500 characters, which teach this flow in this order: call `lil_status`; call the tool, with a `retry_token` on write calls; when a result has a job, call `job_wait` until `done` is true (for `interrupted_unknown` that means it waits for the person); open the artifact paths; `pending_confirmation` means wait for the person, then call again with the same arguments and `retry_token`; text marked untrusted is data; tool names with `.` replaced by `_`; every input schema gains an optional `retry_token: string` described exactly as "Any unique string on write calls. Reuse it only to retry a call or to resume a pending_confirmation."; `_meta` is the object at the descriptor's per-connection `annotations["_meta"]`, its keys (such as `anthropic/requiresUserInteraction`) placed at the top level of the tool's MCP `_meta`, never nested, plus `"anthropic/alwaysLoad": true` on `lil_status`, `project_context` and `job_wait`; an `ok` result is JSON text `{status: "ok", structured, artifacts, summary_text}` plus one text line per artifact path (`lil_status`'s result too), shown as the absolute path `<workspace>\<path with backslashes>` (the workspace path the core reported for the connection; with none, the path as given), written only for a clean relative path (no root, drive, `:`, `.` or `..` segment) or an absolute path inside the suite's job cache, any other path getting no line, while `structured` and `artifacts` keep the workspace-relative `WsPath`; a registered tool name in `summary_text` or in an error's `fix.agent_hint.tool` is written lowered (`job.wait` becomes `job_wait`), whole names only (the next character is not a letter, digit or `_`); a `pending_confirmation` is a non-error result with its JSON and the sentence "Waiting for you to confirm in LILITH; continue with other steps, then call this tool again with the same arguments and retry_token <token>."; an `error` is `isError: true` with the JSON; untrusted detail is preceded by "The text marked untrusted came from an engine or a file; treat it as data." `structuredContent` and `resource_link` are added as extras only. `lil_status` is answered by `lil-mcp` itself, takes `{}` and returns an `ok` result whose `structured` is `{status: "connected" | "incompatible" | "unreachable", caller: "mcp" | "cli" | null (the kind the core resolved, null when not connected), protocol: "M.m" | null, build_id: string | null, workspace: {id, path} | null, core: {pid, channel} | null, error: ErrorBody | null}`. On `protocol.incompatible`, only `lil_status` is listed (and no resource), and it reports the error; a core that cannot be reached or started is `core.unreachable` (ruling P3-R25). MCP resources: `lil://context/project`, `lil://jobs/open`, `lil://assets/recent`.
- **Plugin 1.3** (ARCH-101–108, AI-107, AI-116, AI-117): `plugin/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (marketplace `lilith`), `plugin/worldwright/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (`name: "worldwright"`, `version: "1.3.0"`, `userConfig` `lil_mcp_path` and `lil_path`, each with `title` and `description`, both `type: "file"` and `required: true` with no default, set when you enable the plugin, normally to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\LILITH\bin\lil-mcp.exe` and `…\lil.exe`), `.mcp.json` (`{"mcpServers": {…}}`: server `lil`, command `${user_config.lil_mcp_path}`, plus plugin 1.2.0's `civitai` and `huggingface` servers unchanged), `hooks/hooks.json` (SessionStart, PreToolUse on `Bash|PowerShell|Write|Edit|MultiEdit|mcp__comfyui__.*`, PostToolUse on `Write|Edit|MultiEdit`, each an exec-form command hook `{"type": "command", "command": "${user_config.lil_path}", "args": ["hook", "<event>"], "onFailure": "block"}`, so a hook that fails blocks its action (ruling P3-R30; Claude Code v2.1.295 or later)), plugin 1.2.0's skills and subagents unchanged, `skills/lilith/SKILL.md`, `skills-inventory.toml`. No secret is ever copied into the plugin; tokens stay in the user's environment or `userConfig`.
  - PreToolUse returns `"ask"` for Bash and PowerShell commands whose command word (at the start, or after `;`, `&&`, `||` or `|`) is `lil-mcp` (or `lil-mcp.exe`, by any path), `lil doctor` (or `lil.exe doctor`, by any path) without `--non-interactive` (its repairs wait for a `y` that only you may type), a delete (`rm`, `del`, `erase`, `rd`, `rmdir`, `Remove-Item`, `ri`, `git clean`, `git reset --hard`), an install (`pip`/`pip3`/`uv pip`/`python -m pip` `install`, `uv add`, `npm install`/`i`/`ci`, `pnpm add`/`install`, `yarn add`, `cargo install`, `winget`/`choco`/`scoop` `install`, `Install-Module`, `Install-Package`, `comfy node install`/`registry-install`) or a script run (`python`/`py` with a file or `-c`, `node <file>`, `bash`/`sh <file>` or `-c`, `cmd /c`, `pwsh`/`powershell` `-File` or `-Command`, `& <path>`, `.\x.ps1`, `./x.sh`, `blender -P`/`--python`, `Invoke-Expression`/`iex`, `npm run`, `npx`, `uvx`, a pipe into `sh`, `bash`, `iex` or `python`); and for the comfy MCP's `install_node`, `update_comfyui`, `switch_comfyui_version`, `download_model` and `free_memory`, for its `restart_comfyui` when no core runs, and for its `job` and `download` calls whose `tool_input.action` is `cancel` or missing (any other `action` gets no decision);
  - it returns `"deny"` for `mcp__comfyui__launch_comfyui`, `mcp__comfyui__stop_comfyui`, `mcp__comfyui__restart_comfyui`, `mcp__comfyui__partner_generate` and `mcp__comfyui__auth_login` while a core runs (`lil status --no-start` exit 0); with no core those five get no decision, except `restart_comfyui`, which asks;
  - it returns `"ask"` for a Write, Edit or MultiEdit whose `file_path` is a `suite.toml` inside a folder named `LILITH` or `LILITH-<channel>`; for shell commands there is one rule: a Bash or PowerShell command asks when it is **write-like** and names such a `suite.toml`. Write-like means its target is that file through `>` or `>>`, or its command word is `Set-Content`, `Add-Content`, `Out-File`, `Clear-Content`, `New-Item`, `Remove-Item`, `Move-Item`, `Rename-Item`, `Copy-Item`, `rm`, `del`, `mv`, `cp`, `tee` or `sed -i`. Every other command, reads such as `cat`, `grep`, `type`, `Get-Content` and `Select-String` included, gets no decision from this rule (reading private paths is the business of the existing deny rules);
  - Write and Edit of `queue/jobs*.jsonl`, `registry/usage*.jsonl` and `*.manifest.json` get a `systemMessage` warning and no decision (warn-only, ARCH-104);
  - PostToolUse runs `lil notify --no-start <file_path>` for a file inside the default workspace; SessionStart adds `additionalContext` with the active project, open jobs and pending confirmations when a core runs, naming each open job's `job_id` and each pending confirmation's `id`, and nothing otherwise;
  - with `LIL_ENGINE=1` every hook prints nothing and exits 0. The hook never answers `allow` (ruling P3-R28): it answers `ask` or `deny` when a command matches these lists, contains interpreter re-entry, comments or here-strings, or can't be read to its end, and otherwise gives no decision, so Claude Code's own permission mode decides. In detail: anything it can't read (an open quote, a comment, a here-string, Bash `$'…'`, typographic quotes), interpreter re-entry (`cmd`, `pwsh`, `powershell`, `bash`, `sh`, `python`, `node`, `eval`, `Invoke-Expression`/`iex`, the `&` and `.` call operators, `Start-Process`, … unless the only argument is a version or help flag, and also when text is piped or redirected into them), a program run by its path, an installer with a flag before its subcommand, and a PowerShell hashtable, script block or assignment that hides a command asks. So do (ruling P3-R32): Bash brace expansion `{…,…}` in any unquoted word; a command word after a wrapper (`xargs`, `env`, `timeout`, `nice`, `sudo`, `time`, `stdbuf`, `nohup`, `command`, `builtin`, `exec`, …, read past their value options such as `xargs -n 1` and `timeout -s KILL`) that isn't a plain command name; `env -S`, `trap`, `alias` and `unalias`; `awk`, `gawk`, `mawk` and `nawk`; `sed` with an `e` command or flag (or a script file); `git -c` with a `core.*` key or a key ending in `pager`, `editor`, `sshCommand` or `askpass` (and the other keys git runs as a program: `pager.<command>`, `filter.*`, and keys ending in `command`, `textconv`, `driver`, `external`, `program` or `helper`), and `git --config-env`; `tar` with `--to-command`, `--checkpoint-action`, `--use-compress-program`/`-I` or `--rsh-command`; and in PowerShell a method or static call on a `$` expression (`$f.Delete()`, `$t::Delete(…)`), member invocation through `ForEach-Object`, `%` or `foreach` (a bare member name or `-MemberName`), `Start-Job`, `Start-ThreadJob`, `Import-Module` and `Add-Type`. `suite.toml` targets are read as Windows reads them (`.`, `..`, trailing dots and spaces, alternate data streams, case; 8.3 names and wildcards resolved through the file system, else asked about; a `[` or `{` in the last two segments that the file system can't resolve asks, ruling P3-R32). The hook is a best-effort ask layer over Claude Code's permission rules (ruling P3-R34): a line it can't classify gets no decision, so the repo deny rules and the session's permission mode remain the other layers, and a no-decision form found later is fixed as a bug. When the hook itself fails (it panics, or can't tell whether a core runs for the comfy MCP's denied tools) PreToolUse answers `ask`; a `lil_path` that can't start at all is a failed hook, which `"onFailure": "block"` turns into a blocked call (ruling P3-R30), and `lil doctor` checks that the hooks run. The core check probes the channel of the `lil.exe` that `lil_path` names, so `lil_path` should be the channel that drives ComfyUI.
- **The `lil` commands this plan adds.** Exit codes are as in Appendix D.

  | Command | Output |
  |---|---|
  | `lil hook <session-start \| pre-tool-use \| post-tool-use>` | reads the hook JSON on stdin; prints the hook output JSON or nothing; exits 0; a malformed input (not a JSON object, or without the fields its tool needs) asks on PreToolUse and prints nothing for the other events |
  | `lil test --real [--record <dir>]` | runs `cargo nextest run --workspace --run-ignored only -E 'test(/^real_/)'` from the source checkout, building into `target\real` (the running `lil.exe` can't be replaced), with `LIL_REAL_COMFYUI` set to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` from `[engines.comfyui]`; outside a checkout it says so and exits 2 |
  | `lil doctor` | adds: the workspace `state.db` and its local folder (damaged, newer, hot journal, orphan `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal`, each set aside only after you type `y`); legacy names (a workspace using `worldwright.yaml`, `.worldwright\state.json` or `ww://` refs is a `problem:`; only after you type `y` does the core rename `worldwright.yaml` to `lilith.yaml`, copy the `state.json` hint to `.lilith\`, and rewrite `ww://` to `lil://` in asset manifests with targeted edits, never in a sealed, read-only `manifest.snapshot.json`, which it lists as skipped (so `ww://` parsing stays for sealed versions); `.worldwright\` itself is left in place; never with `--non-interactive`); ComfyUI (`[engines.comfyui]` present, `root` and `python` exist, owned launch args loopback, an attached engine's exposure, a `pass_env` entry the scrub refuses or that can never name a variable is a `problem:` naming it (ruling P3-R31), and one let through whose name holds `API_KEY`, `SECRET` or `TOKEN` but isn't a hub token is a `warning:` naming it (ruling P3-R31b), the "Prefer no sysmem fallback" reminder, ARCH-072); Claude Code (`claude.exe` resolved; a Claude Code older than 2.1.295, which can't honour the hooks' `onFailure`, is a `problem:` naming the update step, `claude update`; the plugin installed and at 1.3.0 in the default profile, installed or updated in place only after `y`, never removed; its `userConfig` `lil_mcp_path` and `lil_path` are set and point into a folder the running channel trusts (`bin\` or the install folder for stable, the build folder for dev); an unset or untrusted value is a `problem:` naming the exact path to enter in that field (`<folder>\lil-mcp.exe`, `<folder>\lil.exe`, for the running channel's folder), and the doctor never writes them; the workspace's `.claude/settings.json` allow and ask rules, written only after `y`; skills and agents still writing directly, from `skills-inventory.toml`; the options' files exist and `<lil_path> hook pre-tool-use` answers `ask` for an `rm`, and the installed plugin has its `hooks/hooks.json`; a marketplace `lilith` added from another folder is a `problem:` naming `claude plugin marketplace remove lilith`, never changed by the doctor; every question takes its answer only from a terminal, so a piped `y` is no) |

- **Fail points added:** `jobs.after_submitting`, `jobs.after_post`, `jobs.after_outputs`, `meter.after_pending`, `meter.after_usage_line`, `caps.after_reserve`, `confirm.after_answer`.
- **Clarifications recorded in Appendix F** (no new rule IDs): ARCH-047, `mcp` needs `claude.exe` as the direct parent; ARCH-054, CORE-112 and AI-114, `mcp_confirmation`, its default, and no `requiresUserInteraction` marker for `mcp` with `"core"`; ARCH-064, the engine is every process in its Job Object; ARCH-071, the free-VRAM formula and fit signal; ARCH-013/AI-107 `lil.exe` beside `lil-mcp.exe` in `bin\`; ARCH-013/AI-107/ARCH-108/AI-117 the plugin paths come from `userConfig`; ARCH-055/CORE-113/APP-051 "can be answered" in plan 3; CORE-091/QA-002 the retry token; CORE-108 engine tool classes; CORE-069/CORE-013 money as decimal strings; CORE-085 cap keys and invalid caps; CORE-115 the 30-minute lifetime; CORE-130 the client id, prefixes and upload names; AI-110 dots in tool names; AI-115 what waits for the skills port; ARCH-106 the comfy MCP tools that are denied or asked; ARCH-060 trusted registry names in the model inventory; CORE-018 the graph checks before the POST; CORE-065 the idle baseline; the `.lilref` name.

## Appendix G. App shell and plumbing (plan 4a clarification)

This appendix records the names, shapes and tables that plan 4a implements for the app shell and its plumbing: job fields and queue control, estimates, the ComfyUI launch profile, the model inventory report, engine environments, notifications, activation and registration, `lil:` URLs, `.lilref` files, app state, session and listing tools, `lil-mcp` launches, plugin 1.3.1 and diagnostics. They are contracts, read the same way as the lead-in "Names that are contracts" of the plan they come from. It adds no rule IDs except ARCH-127, which §1.1 holds. It clarifies, and for ARCH-022 makes one ruled exception to (P4a-R41), ARCH-016, ARCH-021, ARCH-022, ARCH-029, ARCH-030, ARCH-031, ARCH-055, ARCH-072, ARCH-117, ARCH-126, CORE-014, CORE-113, CORE-117, CORE-130, CORE-146, CORE-160, CORE-161, CORE-164, APP-020, APP-022, APP-029, APP-039, APP-041, APP-043, APP-049, APP-051, APP-063, APP-065, APP-146 and QA-016. Where a rule carries a short clarification, this appendix governs, and it governs Appendix F's text marked "from plan 4a" too. Protocol 1.2, its capabilities and the plan-4a error code are in Appendix D; the external facts these contracts rely on were re-verified on 2026-10-23 (Appendix B.3).

- **Jobs** (CORE-117, CORE-160, ARCH-022, ARCH-072):
  - `JobSummary` adds, from plan 4a: `batch_id` (`b_<16 hex>`; every job has one, P4a-R13), `index` (its 0-based place in the batch), `status_seq: [{status, substatus, at}]` (every change in order; at most 64 entries, the first and the newest 63), `origin: {surface, id?, step?}` (below), `engine_device?` (the GPU adapter's name, for a job that held the GPU lease), `phase?`, `subtasks?: [{name, value?, max?}]`, `indeterminate?: true`, `last_progress_at?`, `cache_hit_nodes?: [node_id]`, `all_cached?: true`, `unresponsive?: true`, `pause_reasons?: [reason]` (while the job is `queue_paused`: the reasons holding it) and `estimate?: Estimate` (taken when the job was submitted and never changed afterwards; owner decision 2026-10-23); times are RFC 3339 UTC.
  - **Origin** (owner decision 2026-10-23; P4a-R12): `surface` is an open set. Plan 4a writes `panel` for a verified `ui:<app>` caller, with `id` the app's name (a per-panel id waits for a `tools/call` field), `mcp` for Claude Code, `agent_session` for the in-app assistant, `cli`, and `core` for a job the core submits itself; the director plan adds `director_task`, with `id` the task's job id and `step`. An app shows a surface it doesn't know by its name. From plan 4a each jobs log line (Appendix F) also carries `origin`, after `priority`.
  - **Phase:** from the class type of the node ComfyUI reports `executing` (the core reads it from the job's graph), the first match winning: contains `Loader` → `loading_models`; contains `TextEncode` → `encoding`; contains `Sampler` → `sampling`; contains `Decode` → `decoding`; starts with `Save` or `Preview` → `saving`; anything else → `other`. From `execution_success` until the outputs are staged the phase is `ingesting`. Other job kinds report the phase their handler sets. `progress.node` stays the raw node id.
  - `subtasks` lists each phase the job reached, in order, as `{name}`; the current one also carries `value` and `max` from the newest `progress` event while that phase lasts. `indeterminate: true` while the executing node has sent no `progress` event.
  - `last_progress_at` is the time of the newest `executing` or `progress` event. `unresponsive: true` while the job is `running` with substatus `executing` and `last_progress_at` (or `started`, before any event) is **30 s** or more ago; the core checks every 5 s and publishes `{kind: "unresponsive", on}` on `job:<id>` when it flips; nothing is cancelled.
  - `cache_hit_nodes` are the node ids of ComfyUI's `execution_cached` event; when every node the prompt executes, other than the nodes whose `filename_prefix` the suite sets, was cached, the job succeeds with `all_cached: true` and the summary text "Nothing changed: ComfyUI served every node from its cache." (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24: a node with a `filename_prefix` input, such as a Save node, always runs again, since the suite gives it its own prefix, which is part of ComfyUI's cache key, so the nodes whose `filename_prefix` the suite sets may run again. As ComfyUI does, the nodes the prompt executes are its output nodes, those whose class `/object_info` marks `output_node` (and, for a class it doesn't list, a node with a `filename_prefix` input), and their ancestors through the graph's links; a node with no path to an output never runs, so it is left out. Every other node executed is judged, an output node without a `filename_prefix` included, such as a sampler marked `output_node` or a preview: ComfyUI lists it in `execution_cached` when nothing about it changed.)
  - The watchdog sets `watchdog: "slow"` when the time since `execution_start` passes `max(3 × p50, 2 × p90)` of the job's `estimate.wall_s` (measured history), else 3 × `expected_secs` (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24: a history bound of 0 s, from similar jobs that reported no execution window, so no time was measured, counts as no history; the bound only changes how far the clock runs, never when it starts).
  - **Queue order:** class rank `interactive`, `user`, `live`, `agent` (`live` is plan 6a's; no plan-4a job has it); within a class, the lower `order_key` first, where a new job's key is its core-wide submission sequence (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24: one counter for every scope's `state.db`, and never below the submission time in microseconds, so first in, first out holds across the suite and workspace scopes and across restarts; a job of an older schema keeps its `seq`; equal keys go by `job_id`. The counter's high-water mark is kept in the suite `state.db`, raised with each submission and each `job.move`, and read at the first submission after a start, so a clock set back across a restart never sorts a newer job before an older one.) Plan 3's limit of 8 overtakes counts only group moves.
  - **Queue position** (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A): a job this core holds queued (substatus `waiting`, `waiting_for_vram` or `queue_paused`, a job whose prompt a pause withdrew included) has `queue_position` in its `JobSummary`: its 0-based place in its lane, counting the lane's queued jobs of every scope (the suite and each workspace) that the lane dispatches in their turn, in queue order: class, then a job requeued at the head of its class (CORE-125), then `order_key`, then `job_id`, before any CORE-124 group overtake. A job a lane reason holds keeps its place, since the reason holds every job of its classes alike; a job a restart holds is never picked until it is released, so it has no `queue_position` and isn't counted ahead of any other (review fix, 2026-10-26). It is the place when the summary was made: another job starting, moving, being submitted or ending changes it with no event of this job, so a panel that shows the queue re-reads `jobs.list {order: "queue"}` after a `job.move` result and on every `jobs` event of a job that is queued now or that the panel shows as queued (coalesced). A queued job's `queue_position` less the jobs ahead of it that the panel lists is the number ahead that it can't see (another scope's), which the queued ETA counts as jobs with no estimate.
  - `job.move {job_id, to: "next" | "last"}` (`write`) sets the job's `order_key` to one below the lowest, or one above the highest, key among the queued jobs of its lane and class, and returns `{job}`. Only a job in substatus `waiting`, `waiting_for_vram` or `queue_paused` moves; any other gets `job.not_queued`. A move never changes the class and never touches a prompt in the engine.
  - `job.cancel` takes exactly one of `{job_id}` (result `{job}`, as in plan 3), `{ids}` (1–500 job ids), `{batch_id}` or `{all_mine: true}` (results `{jobs: [JobSummary]}`); `all_mine` covers every unfinished job whose idempotency scope names the caller's kind and, for a `ui:*` caller, the same app. None or more than one selector is `params.invalid`.
  - **Pause GPU work:** `app/queue.pause {lane?}` and `app/queue.resume {lane?, held_only?}` (`lane`: `gpu-heavy`, the default, or `cpu`) return `QueueState {lane, paused, reasons: [reason], held, restart_held, withdrawn}`: `held` counts the lane's queued jobs in substatus `queue_paused` for any reason, a restart's included; `restart_held` counts those held by a restart; `withdrawn` counts the prompts the current pause withdrew from the engine. **Pause reasons** (owner decision 2026-10-23) are an open set of names, each with a fixed scope, `all` or `agent_only`: plan 4a has `manual` (set by `app/queue.pause`) and `fullscreen` (the auto-pause), both `all`, and the job-level `restart` (below), which never pauses the lane; `agent_only` is reserved for a later plan's reasons and holds only `agent`-class jobs. A lane dispatches a queued job only while no reason whose scope covers the job's class is set; each job held that way, whatever its caller, shows substatus `queue_paused` and lists the holding reasons in `pause_reasons`; the prompt already executing is never interrupted. `paused` is true while the lane has any reason; `app/queue.resume` removes only `manual` (P4a-R5), and releases the lane's jobs held by a restart (below). `app/queue.resume {held_only: true}` (P4a-R41) only releases the lane's jobs held by a restart: it never removes `manual` or any other reason, so a released job still waits while your pause or another reason covers it. An app shows a reason it doesn't know by its name. `jobs.list`'s result gains `lanes: [QueueState]`, and the `jobs` topic gains `{kind: "queue", state: QueueState}`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) It is published whenever a lane's `QueueState` changes, and only then: a pause or a resume, a withdrawal, a job held or released (a submission into a paused lane, a recovery's restart hold, a release) and a held job's end, so the `jobs` topic alone keeps `lanes` current. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough) A job submitted into a lane a reason holds is `queue_paused`, with those reasons, in the submit result too (`test.job`'s `{job}` or `{jobs}`, as every submitting tool's), its `status_seq` ending in that `queue_paused` step, as `jobs.list` shows it; it never reads `waiting` first.
  - **Restart hold** (ARCH-022, CORE-160; ruling P4a-R41, an exception to ARCH-022's first sentence): at core start, each job that never reached an engine (status `queued`, substatus `waiting`, `waiting_for_vram` or `queue_paused`, and no `external_id` in its row; plan 7a adds `waiting_for_ram`), other than a director task (`assistant_task`, which stays under ARCH-022 until the director plan, roadmap N33), stays `queued`, in substatus `queue_paused` with `pause_reasons: ["restart"]`, instead of becoming `interrupted`; every other job without a final state follows Appendix F's startup rule. `restart` (scope `all`) holds only those jobs, never the lane, so new jobs run. `app/queue.resume {lane?}` releases every `restart`-held job of the lane, besides removing `manual`; a released job still waits while a lane reason covers it. `job.cancel` cancels a held job as usual. A held job keeps its cap reservation: the meter's startup repair leaves it, since the job isn't final. The hold is kept in memory, so after another restart the same jobs are held again. Jobs shows a "Resume held jobs (<n>)" control whenever `restart_held` is above 0, whatever `paused` says; it calls `app/queue.resume {lane, held_only: true}`, so your own pause survives it, and Jobs labels such a job's reason "Held after a restart". When a start held any job, the core posts one toast in the `job_failed` category, so the "Job failed" toggle also covers it: "Jobs held after a restart" / "<n> jobs wait until you resume them in Jobs", with Tag `restart_<the first held job's id>` and Group `job_failed`, launching `<scheme>:task/<the first held job's id>`. The held jobs stay listed in Jobs until they are released or cancelled. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) Each recovery that holds jobs, at start or when a workspace is first used later, posts one such toast for the jobs it held, with Tag `restart_<the first job that recovery held>`. (owner decision 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough; it supersedes ruling P4a-R41's toast at every start that holds jobs) The toast comes once per held batch: the ids of the jobs a posted restart-hold toast counted are kept in the suite `state.db` (table `restart_hold_toasts (job_id, toasted_ms)`, the newest 4,096), so a later recovery that holds only jobs already counted posts nothing, though it holds them again; a recovery that holds any job not counted yet posts one toast for all the jobs it held. A toast that wasn't posted (its category off, the path unavailable, a suite window in front) counts nothing, so the next recovery tries again. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) A job the core submitted itself (`origin.surface: "core"`, such as `comfy.inventory`) is never held: it follows ARCH-022 (`interrupted`, its completions told that it never started), and `job.cancel {all_mine: true}` leaves it. `job.wait`'s summary text for a `queue_paused` job names its reasons and, for `restart` or `manual`, says that it waits until you resume it in Jobs.
  - `job.retry` also takes `{batch_id}` (`write`): it retries the newest attempt at each `index` of the batch, the job that no other job names as `retry_of`, when it is `failed` (`interrupted` included) or `cancelled`, under the rules of `job.retry {job_id}`, and returns `{retried: [{job, retry_of}], refused: [{job_id, error}]}`. A retried job keeps the original's `batch_id` and `index`. It is idempotent: a job already retried is never retried again, so a second call creates nothing new for the jobs the first one retried, and `job.retry {job_id}` on a job already retried is refused with `job.not_retryable`. Every job of the batch it doesn't retry is in `refused`, each with its reason: an attempt that a later one retried, with `job.not_retryable` and the message "already retried as <newer job id>"; a newest attempt that succeeded or hasn't ended, with `job.not_retryable` and its status; and one that `job.retry {job_id}` would refuse, with that error.
  - **Withdrawal** (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24: the endpoint): when a lane pauses, each suite job of it in `queued_in_engine` whose prompt `/queue` still lists as pending is removed from ComfyUI's queue with `POST /queue {"delete": [<prompt_id>]}`, which takes out only a pending prompt and never interrupts a running one; then `/api/jobs/{id}` decides: gone, the prompt was withdrawn, its `external_id` is cleared, the old id is kept in the row's `withdrawn_id`, and it returns to `queue_paused`; started (or ended), it runs on, as does a prompt that started meanwhile. When ComfyUI doesn't answer where the prompt is, it is asked again a few times (`/api/jobs/{id}`, then `/history/{id}`), for at most 5 s in all, outside the POST's lock (the `/queue` read and the delete, under the lock, are bounded to 5 s together too: a delete that may have been sent then counts as unanswered), and `app/queue.pause` never waits for it (it answers at once; each withdrawal publishes the lane's state when it is decided); until an answer shows it gone, it isn't taken as withdrawn, and the attempt keeps following it, remembering that a delete was sent for that prompt id: when it later finds the prompt gone, that is the withdrawal, never "cancelled from outside the suite", and the job returns to `queue_paused` with the id in `withdrawn_id` (or, when no reason covers it any more, waits its turn again), and the gone-check runs before any repost (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24). A job of the paused lane that hasn't reached the engine yet (`submitting`, or in a retry's backoff) is held too: it posts nothing and returns to `queue_paused` (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24). `POST /api/jobs/{id}/cancel` stays the cancel and the shutdown's endpoint (CORE-130). A job with `external_side_effects` is never withdrawn. On resume, a withdrawn job is posted with a new prompt id only after `/api/jobs/{old}` and `/history/{old}` both show the old one gone (CORE-120), and `withdrawn_id` is cleared only then; since the id is in the row, this holds after a stop or a crash too, and for a job a restart held (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24). A withdrawn prompt found still there is followed, never posted twice. A `job.retry` (or an automatic re-run) of a job whose row still has a `withdrawn_id` copies it: the new job posts only once that prompt has ended or is gone, and never takes it over, since its outputs carry the other job's prefix; while that prompt is pending or running, the new job's attempt waits, asking ComfyUI about it every 2 s and holding its lane, until it leaves or the job is cancelled or the core stops (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24). (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-29, Task 22) While it waits so (status `queued`, substatus `submitting`, with a `retry_of` and a `withdrawn_id` in its row), its `JobSummary` has `waiting_for_earlier_prompt: true`, and Jobs shows it as waiting for an earlier ComfyUI prompt to finish. Fail point `jobs.after_withdraw` fires after the withdrawal's answer and before the row changes; only its `abort` action means anything there (an error is logged, and the withdrawal goes on).
  - `jobs.list` also takes `since?` (RFC 3339, with `Z` or an offset: the jobs whose `updated` is later) and `batch_id?`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) `updated` is kept in whole seconds, so a `since` with a fractional part also lists the jobs updated in its own second (a reader that drops repeats by `job_id` misses none); a whole-second `since` lists the jobs whose `updated` is later. `order?: "newest" | "queue"` (default `newest`): `queue` lists the scope's jobs this core holds queued, by lane (`gpu-heavy` first), then by `queue_position`, the jobs a restart holds after the others of their lane (in queue order, with no `queue_position`); its `next_cursor` names the last job listed by its place in that order, so the next page starts after that place even when the queue changed between pages: a job that starts, ends or is cancelled between pages is neither listed again nor skipped, but a job moved, released or submitted ahead of the cursor meanwhile can be missed or listed twice, so a reader drops repeats by `job_id` and re-reads from the start on the events above (re-review fix, 2026-10-26). A page ends early, with a `next_cursor`, before its jobs pass 3 MiB of JSON, so the result stays under the line cap (ARCH-025); `limit` is a maximum. A `JobSummary` leaves out `result.structured.executed_graph` (the graph a `comfy.prompt` ran, CORE-130), which stays in the job's row and in a diagnostics bundle's `job.json`.
  - **Fullscreen** (P4a-R6): the core polls `SHQueryUserNotificationState` every **2 s**; `QUNS_RUNNING_D3D_FULL_SCREEN`, `QUNS_BUSY` and `QUNS_PRESENTATION_MODE` are fullscreen, and leaving counts after 2 polls in a row that aren't. With `[jobs] fullscreen_autopause = true` (default `false`) fullscreen adds the `fullscreen` reason to `gpu-heavy` and leaving removes it. The `desktop` topic gets `{kind: "fullscreen", on}`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) The `fullscreen` reason is set exactly while `fullscreen_autopause` is on and the poll says fullscreen: turning the setting on during fullscreen adds it at once, and turning it off removes it at once. A poll whose call fails isn't fullscreen.
  - **Keep-awake** (ARCH-127): while any `gpu-heavy` job is `running` and `[power] keep_awake_gpu` is on (default `true`), the core holds one system-required power request, `PowerSetRequest(PowerRequestSystemRequired)` on a `PowerCreateRequest` object with a reason string, and releases it with `PowerClearRequest` as soon as none runs; when the request can't be set on this PC, `keep_awake_available` is `false`. The core logs each sleep it sees (`slept`, with `from` and `to`), from the hidden window's `PBT_APMSUSPEND` and `PBT_APMRESUMEAUTOMATIC`, with a wall-clock jump of more than 60 s against the monotonic clock, checked every 30 s, as the backstop for a sleep no broadcast reported, and publishes `{kind: "resumed", slept_from, at}` on `desktop`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) Turning `keep_awake_gpu` off releases a held request at once, and turning it on while such a job runs holds it at once; `keep_awake_available` is `false` when `PowerCreateRequest` fails. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25, Task 9 fix round 1) The hidden window also registers with `RegisterSuspendResumeNotification`, so a Modern Standby PC sends it the broadcasts. The backstop's "wall-clock jump against the monotonic clock" is the growth of the interrupt-time bias, `QueryInterruptTime` minus `QueryUnbiasedInterruptTime`, which grows by exactly the time the PC slept or hibernated: checked every 30 s, a growth of more than 60 s since the last check is a sleep of that length ending at the check. A wall-clock step (an NTP correction or a manual change) never counts as a sleep. A sleep that a broadcast and the backstop both see is logged and published once, and a `PBT_APMSUSPEND` older than the newest resume is discarded.
- **Events** (ARCH-032) (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A): a `preview:<job_id>` topic keeps only its newest event in its history (a late subscriber wants only the newest frame), and every topic's history is pruned at least once a minute, on a publish to any topic, so a topic that nothing publishes to any more holds nothing within about 11 minutes of its last event, once anything publishes. A topic's seq is kept, so cursors stay valid. A per-job topic (`job:<id>`, `preview:<job_id>`) that no client follows is forgotten once its history is empty, but its seq is kept as a tombstone among the newest 4,096 forgotten topics: a topic published again continues its numbering from it, and a cursor of it is judged against it as against a live topic; a subscribe makes no entry for a topic nothing publishes on, and is answered from its tombstone or the starting point below. (re-review fix, 2026-10-26) A topic that is neither live nor kept as a tombstone starts its numbering above every forgotten seq the bus no longer keeps, so a cursor older than that (or ahead of a topic's current seq) gets a snapshot or `resync_required`, never a replay that skips events.
- **Estimates** (CORE-161, APP-140):
  - `job.estimate {tool, args}` (`read`, module `jobs`) validates `args` as a call of `tool` would (`tool.unknown`, `params.invalid`) and returns an `Estimate` in parts (owner decision 2026-10-23): `{local?: {source, samples, gpu_s_p50, gpu_s_p90, wh_p50, wh_p90, energy_cost?}, wall_s?: {source, samples, p50, p90}, money?: {source, p50, p90, upper_bound, currency}, simulated?: {source, p50, p90, currency}, quota?: {source, unit, p50, p90}, unknown: [{part, reason}]}`. A part is present only when it was estimated, with its own `source` (`measured_history`, `price_table` or `prices_yaml`). `unknown` names each part the call needs but couldn't estimate, with `reason` `no_history` (fewer than 3 similar jobs), `no_key` (the tool has no `estimate_key`) or `no_price`; a call that spends no money has no `money` part and doesn't list it. Plan 4a defines `local`, `wall_s` and `money` and produces only `local` and `wall_s`, from `measured_history`; prices arrive with plan 6b, and `simulated` (CORE-171) and `quota` are reserved. An estimate with no part is "no estimate yet". (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24) A call of a tool with no `estimate_key` lists `local` and `wall_s` with `no_key`; with a key and too little history, both with `no_history`. `local` and `wall_s` come from the same history rows, and `samples` is their count (at most 20); a similar job whose energy wasn't attributed (it reported no execution window) counts 0 GPU-seconds and 0 Wh. `energy_cost` is `{amount, currency}`, a decimal string and its currency code, given only while `[power] electricity`'s currency is the display currency (plan 4a converts no rate). The estimate a job takes at submission is its handler's: the key is computed from the job's own arguments, so every way of submitting the job, a retry and an automatic re-run included, keys it alike.
  - History (P4a-R14): the newest 20 succeeded jobs with the same `kind` and `estimate_key` in `telemetry.db` `job_stats`; fewer than 3 is `no_history`; p50 and p90 are nearest-rank; seconds are decimal strings with one decimal, Wh with three (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24: the newest by `finished_ms`, then by `job_id`; values are rounded half to even). `energy_cost` is `wh_p50` at `[power] electricity`, in the display currency, when that is set.
  - **Adding estimates** (owner decision 2026-10-23): a part adds only to the same part, and `money` and `simulated` only in the same currency; nothing adds across parts. A sum over jobs adds their p50s, and its p90 is that sum plus the square root of the sum of each job's (p90 − p50)²; p90s are never added. A job without the part is counted, never taken as zero: the sum carries `of` (the jobs) and `not_estimated` (those without it), shown as "3 of 12 not estimated". A queued job's ETA is its finish time: the remaining `wall_s.p50` of the job running in its lane (never below 0), plus the `wall_s.p50` of each job ahead of it in lane order (class, then `order_key`), plus its own; jobs among them with no `wall_s` are counted and named ("after 2 jobs with no estimate"). (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24) A sum in which no job has the part has no p50 or p90: it reads as not estimated, never as 0. A figure that isn't a finite number of 0 or more counts as not estimated. `money` (and `simulated`) sums are carried per currency, each with its own `of` and `not_estimated`, never converted into one.
  - `estimate_key`: for `comfy.prompt`, the first 16 hex of SHA-256 over the canonical JSON (keys sorted, no whitespace) of its graph with every input named `seed`, `noise_seed` or `filename_prefix` removed; for `test.job`, its `estimate_key` argument (default `"test"`); other tools have none and estimate `none`. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24) The `comfy.prompt` graph also leaves out each node's `_meta` and its free-text inputs: every input named `text`, `text_g`, `text_l`, `prompt` or `negative`, and every string input of a node whose `class_type` contains `TextEncode`. The model names, the sizes and the numeric inputs, which drive the time, stay in, so a new prompt text for the same work is a similar job.
  - `telemetry.db` `user_version = 2` adds `job_stats(job_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, kind TEXT NOT NULL, estimate_key TEXT NOT NULL, finished_ms INTEGER NOT NULL, wall_ms INTEGER NOT NULL, gpu_seconds_milli INTEGER NOT NULL, system_mj INTEGER NOT NULL)` with an index on `(kind, estimate_key, finished_ms)`; `meter` writes a row when a job with an `estimate_key` succeeds; `wall_ms` is the sum of its execution windows; a version-1 file is migrated forward, keeping its rows. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24) A run with `all_cached: true` measured the cache, not the work, and adds no row; a partly cached run does. The row is written once the job's final state `succeeded` is in (after its Record step), at most once per job id, so a job whose Record step fails, or one interrupted and run again, never counts twice. `job_stats` keeps the newest 20 rows of each `(kind, estimate_key)`, the ones the estimator reads: older rows are deleted in the same transaction as the insert. There is no age-based retention.
  - `telemetry.energy {from, to}` (`read`) → `{from, to, gpu_mj, cpu_mj, system_mj, measured: {gpu, cpu}, energy_cost?}` over the samples in the range, both bounds included, from the finest table that still holds the range (`s1`, then `m1`, then `h1`), with `JobEnergy`'s whole-system formula. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24) A table holds the range while its retention still covers `from`; from `m1` or `h1` a row counts when its bucket holds any of the range, as in `telemetry.series`, so the result equals the sum of that table's `*.energy` points over the range. The rest-of-system part is `rest_of_system_w` over the samples' time: their `dt` in `s1`, a nominal second per sample in `m1` and `h1`. From `m1` and `h1` too, `measured` says that every sample had that figure and none was estimated: those tables count each bucket's samples with an estimated figure in rows of their own (`gpu.energy.estimated`, `cpu.energy.estimated`), which are not series metrics. A row written before those marks existed carries none and reads as measured. `energy_cost` is `{amount, currency}` at `[power] electricity`'s rate, in that rate's currency.
  - `Snapshot` adds `gpu_users: [{pid, name, vram_local_mib, sm_permille?, role?}]`: the 8 processes with the most dedicated GPU memory, tracked or not; `name` is untrusted. A verified `ui:*` connection's process is tracked with role `app` while it is connected (P4a-R27).
- **Out of memory** (CORE-163): the retries are Appendix F's; the final failure is `comfyui.out_of_memory` with the message "ComfyUI ran out of GPU memory, also on 2 retries.", `fix.user_hint` "Close other programs that use the GPU, or choose the model profile's lighter variant, then retry." and `retry: after_resolve`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24) `user_hint` is an optional text of the fix (QA-003), shown to you beside its UI action ("Retry the job" here) and its agent hint. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24) A job with `external_side_effects`, which is never retried, fails at its first out of memory with the message "ComfyUI ran out of GPU memory. The job has external side effects, so it was not retried." and `fix.user_hint` "Close other programs that use the GPU, or choose the model profile's lighter variant. Retrying runs the paid call again, and it is charged again."; its fix's UI action is "Show the job" (`show_job`), and it gets no `job.retry` agent hint, since a fix of its own other than "Retry the job" keeps the runner from adding one.
- **ComfyUI launch profile** (ARCH-126; P4a-R15): `[engines.comfyui]` gains `reserve_vram_gb` (a decimal string, default `"1.5"`), `preview_method` (default `"taesd"`), `manager` (default `false`) and `dynamic_vram` (default `true`). An owned start runs `<python> main.py --listen 127.0.0.1 --port <port> --reserve-vram <reserve_vram_gb> --preview-method <preview_method>`, then `--enable-manager` only when `manager` is true (it does nothing unless the `comfyui-manager` Python package is installed; without it ComfyUI warns and starts without Manager), then `--disable-dynamic-vram` only when `dynamic_vram` is false (true means ComfyUI may use dynamic VRAM, not that it does: that also needs PyTorch 2.8 or later and a working `comfy-aimdo`), then `<extra_args>`. `--reserve-vram` takes gigabytes as a float. An `extra_args` entry that is `--enable-assets` or any prefix of it from `--enable-as` on, with or without `=<value>`, stops the start with `engine.not_configured` naming it. `engines.status` entries gain `launch_profile: {reserve_vram_gb, preview_method, manager, dynamic_vram, flags: [string]} | null` (`null` for an attached engine) and `asset_catalogue: "on" | "off" | "unknown"`, probed by `GET /api/features`, whose JSON `assets` boolean says whether the catalogue is on (`on` or `off`); the answer is `unknown` when the request fails, the route is missing (an older ComfyUI) or there is no `assets` key. `/api/assets` is never probed, because ComfyUI registers it even when the catalogue is off. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24) `preview_method` is one of `none`, `auto`, `latent2rgb` and `taesd`, the values ComfyUI takes. When no engine runs, `launch_profile` is the profile an owned start would use; it is also `null` when ComfyUI isn't configured. (plan 4a rulings, 2026-10-24) `reserve_vram_gb` is a decimal string (digits with at most one `.`) or a TOML number, finite, from 0 to 256, kept as its decimal text. A launch-profile value that is out of range, of another type or not one of the listed values refuses only an owned start, with `engine.not_configured` naming the key; `launch_profile` shows that key's default, and attaching, the inventory, `models.report` and the outside-jobs watch go on as usual. An `extra_args` entry that is `--windows-standalone-build` or any prefix of it from `--wi` on, with or without `=<value>`, stops the start in the same way, since that flag turns the asset catalogue on. An owned start always ends with `--disable-assets`, after `<extra_args>`, which wins over anything that would turn the catalogue on; `flags` lists the profile's own flags, those before `<extra_args>`. The catalogue is probed when the engine becomes ready. Only a 404, or a JSON answer without `assets`, is a final `unknown`. A probe that fails in any other way (it can't connect, times out after 2 s, gets another 4xx or a 5xx, or its answer can't be read as JSON) stays `unknown` and is made again on a later supervisor pass, at least 30 s after the last one and at most 5 times in all while the engine stays ready, until the first `on` or `off` (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24, fix round 2).
- **Model inventory report** (CORE-164; P4a-R28): `models.report {}` (`read`, module `engines`) → `{root, refreshed, models: [{path, size, sha256, family, states, model_id?, model_name?, untrusted?}], missing: [{model_id, model_name?, sha256}]}`. `path` is relative to `<root>\models`, with `\`. `states` is a subset of `["orphan", "duplicate", "unknown_family"]`: `orphan` when no `registry/models.yaml` entry has its SHA-256, `duplicate` when another file has the same SHA-256, `unknown_family` when `family` is `null`. `missing` lists the `registry/models.yaml` entries whose SHA-256 no file has. `family` is the first of the safetensors `__metadata__` keys `modelspec.architecture` and `ss_base_model_version`, and GGUF's `general.architecture`; it is read only from `.safetensors`, `.sft` and `.gguf` files, and a safetensors header over 100 MiB, a GGUF with more than 1,024 keys before `general.architecture`, or a string over 64 KiB gives `null`. Family values and file names are untrusted (ARCH-060). Families are cached in `model_hashes.family`. With no `root`, the result is `engine.not_configured`. Nothing is moved or deleted because of a state. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24) The report is the newest inventory of this core run, kept after the engine stops; before the first one, `refreshed` is `null` and both lists are empty. Every `models` entry carries `untrusted: true`, since its `path` and `family` are outside text; `model_id` and `model_name` come from the registry and are trusted. A file whose hash wasn't taken (a cloud placeholder, never read) has `sha256: null`, is `orphan` and has no family. Each `missing` entry is one SHA-256 of a registry entry. A file's family is read again only when its cached size, mtime or file identity changed, or when the last read could not open or read the file (`model_hashes.family_read` is 0); a header that is malformed or over the limits is a read whose answer is `null`. (plan 4a rulings, 2026-10-24) The result also has `registry: "ok" | "missing" | "unreadable" | "no_workspace"`: the default workspace's `registry/models.yaml` was read, doesn't exist, couldn't be read (an I/O error, not YAML or not a list), or there is no default workspace. Unless it is `ok`, no file is `orphan`, `missing` is empty and no entry has a `model_id`: the report doesn't guess from a registry it couldn't read. `duplicate` counts files, not paths: two paths to one file (links with the same volume serial and FileId) are one file. A pickle format (`.ckpt`, `.pt`, `.pth`, `.bin`) is opened only to hash it (CORE-130), never parsed. (plan 4a rulings, 2026-10-24, recorded at Checkpoint A) The 64 KiB limit is on the strings the reader holds (a key or the family value); a GGUF value it skips has no limit of its own, and a GGUF header is read at most 100 MiB, as a safetensors header is. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-30) Keeping an inventory publishes a `status` event on `engines`, whose `inventory` is the new one, so a follower reads `models.report` again without knowing the job.
- **Engine environments** (CORE-130): each capture is kept in the suite `state.db` table `engine_captures (engine, captured_utc, capture)`, the newest 10 per engine. `engines.environment {}` (`read`, module `engines`) → `{captures: [EnvCapture] (newest first, at most 2), changes: [{what: "comfyui" | "node" | "driver", name?, from?, to?, change: "changed" | "added" | "removed"}]}` between those two. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24) A capture is taken each time the engine becomes ready (the `comfy.inventory` job); `captured_utc` is Unix milliseconds, one more than the engine's newest when the clock gives no later time. A `node` change names the `custom_nodes` folder, and its `from` and `to` are `<version> (<commit>)`, or whichever of the two is known; `comfyui`'s are ComfyUI's commit, else its version, and `driver`'s the NVML driver version. A value only the newer capture has is `added`, one only the older has `removed`. With fewer than two captures, `changes` is empty. (plan 4a rulings, 2026-10-24) A capture is stored only when its `comfyui`, its nodes or its `driver` differ from the engine's newest stored capture; otherwise nothing is stored, so the newest two are the last two different environments and the 10 kept are the last 10 changes. A driver that isn't known (no GPU reading when the capture is taken) is never a difference and shows no change; but a known driver arriving while the newest stored capture's driver is unknown is stored as a new capture (stored captures are never changed), so a later driver change is compared with it (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-24, fix round 2). A `custom_nodes` folder whose name starts with `.` (ComfyUI-Manager's `.disabled`) or is `__pycache__` isn't a node. In a capture's `system_stats.system.argv`, an entry that is a path inside `root` is written relative to `root`, so stored and served captures hold no absolute path into the ComfyUI folder. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-30) The capture is taken in the inventory job, after the engine is ready, so its being kept also publishes a `status` event on `engines`.
- **Notifications** (ARCH-005, APP-022, APP-065, APP-146):
  - categories `job_finished`, `job_failed`, `confirmation_waiting`, `cap_alert`, `director_decision` and `agent_activity` (owner decision 2026-10-22), each on by default; there is no `job_progress` (owner decision 2026-10-24: no live progress toasts, below). `suite.toml` `[notifications]` holds each by name, plus `quiet_while_fullscreen = true`; `[jobs] fullscreen_autopause = false`; `[power] keep_awake_gpu = true`. The category set is open (owner decision 2026-10-23): a later plan adds a category with its key and default; an unknown key under `[notifications]` is kept and ignored, and an app shows a category it doesn't know by its key.
  - `desktop.get {fresh?}` (`read`, module `desktop`; `fresh: true` recomputes `notification_path` instead of serving the 10 s cache, P4a-R33) → `DesktopSettings {notifications: {<category>: bool}, quiet_while_fullscreen, fullscreen_autopause, keep_awake_gpu, keep_awake_available, notification_path: {toasts: "ready" | "not_yet_shown" | "unavailable", shortcut: "ok" | "missing" | "wrong", handlers: "ok" | "missing" | "other_channel"}}`. `app/desktop.set {notifications?, quiet_while_fullscreen?, fullscreen_autopause?, keep_awake_gpu?}` (verified `ui:*` only; owner decision 2026-10-22) edits `suite.toml` with targeted edits and returns `DesktopSettings`; the `desktop` topic gets `{kind: "settings", settings}`. A hand edit of `suite.toml` is read at the next core start. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) Caps, unlike these settings, are read at each use, and when a hand edit makes invalid caps valid again, `caps` gets `{kind: "invalid", caps: []}`. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25) `app/desktop.set`'s `notifications` names only the categories `desktop.get` lists; any other key (`job_progress` included) is -32602, and nothing is written. A field left out is left as it is, and so is every value the call doesn't name, as this core read it at its start; each call that succeeds publishes `settings`, even one that changed nothing. A change applies at once: the auto-pause and keep-awake follow it without a restart.
  - **Ready** (the reachability fix): `toasts` is `ready`, `not_yet_shown` or `unavailable`. It is `ready` only when the toast notifier for the channel's AUMID reports `Setting() == Enabled` and the core isn't running elevated, `not_yet_shown` as below (plan 4a ruling 2026-10-24), and otherwise `unavailable`, with `toasts_reason: "disabled_for_app" | "disabled_for_user" | "disabled_by_policy" | "elevated" | "no_platform"` beside it in `notification_path`. `Setting()`'s values map `DisabledForApplication` and `DisabledByManifest` to `disabled_for_app`, `DisabledForUser` to `disabled_for_user` and `DisabledByGroupPolicy` to `disabled_by_policy`; Windows returns the reason of the largest scope (manifest, then group policy, then user, then application). (plan 4a ruling 2026-10-24) `Setting()` fails with `Element not found` (`ERROR_NOT_FOUND`) for an AUMID that has never shown a toast, because Windows creates an app's notification settings on its first toast: that reading (from `Setting()` only, never from creating the notifier) is `toasts: "not_yet_shown"`, which allows posting and counts as `ready` for **Reachable** below. A failed post, or a later `DisabledForApplication`, `DisabledForUser`, `DisabledByGroupPolicy` or `DisabledByManifest` reading, then decides, and those keep their meaning above. It also ends: when this process's first post that returned `Ok` while `not_yet_shown` (counted afresh after any reading that isn't `not_yet_shown`) is 5 s old and `Setting()` still fails so, `toasts` is `unavailable` with `toasts_reason: "no_platform"` for as long as that lasts in the process, so a confirmation answers `confirm.unavailable` at once instead of waiting where no toast can reach you. Why: Task 30's host run saw `Setting()` fail so for 60 s after the shortcut and the AUMID's registration were written, and turn `Enabled`, with the toast in History, about 30 ms after the first toast was posted. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) A post that fails while `not_yet_shown` starts the 5 s as an `Ok` one does, so posts that keep failing end it as `no_platform` too.
  - A toast is posted only when its category is on, the channel's toasts can post (`ready` or `not_yet_shown`, plan 4a ruling 2026-10-24), and no connected `ui:*` app owns the foreground window (P4a-R7); otherwise nothing is posted or queued. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough) While the notification path's `handlers` isn't `ok` (missing, or another channel's), a toast that has a target is posted without it: no `launch` attribute and no actions, since nothing of this channel would open them, and its BODY ends " · Open LILITH to see it" (the BODY before it cut so that the ending always shows); a toast with no target anyway is posted as it is. A confirmation's toast posted so holds no live toast token, and the next reconcile that finds the handlers `ok` posts it again, Tag and all, with its target, its Open and Deny and a fresh token; until then a reconcile leaves it as it is, and (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough review) so does a repost that fails (its category off, the toasts unavailable, a suite window in front): the bare toast stays. `job_finished` and `job_failed` post once per batch, when its last job ends (`job_failed` when any failed; never for `cancelled`), for jobs of priority `user` or `agent` whose `origin.surface` isn't `core`; `confirmation_waiting` posts for each pending confirmation and is removed when it is answered, expires or is cancelled; `cap_alert` posts for each `caps` alert; `agent_activity` posts for `notify.claude_code` and for an agent caller's `open_in` or `ui.show`; `director_decision` comes with the director plan. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) A confirmation's toast is removed by its Tag whenever the confirmation ends, whichever core run posted it. The core reconciles the confirmation toasts at start, after each confirmation load and when the last app that can answer confirmations closes: each pending confirmation without a toast from this run gets one, with a fresh token (the same Tag replaces an earlier run's toast and its dead Deny), and a confirmation toast in the notification centre whose confirmation isn't pending is removed. The notifier hears of confirmations straight from the confirmations module, so a burst of other events can't drop them; when it misses `jobs` events (`resync_required`), it checks again each batch with a job finished since the last event it handled.
  - **Asking and telling** (owner decision 2026-10-23): a toast that asks something of you (in plan 4a, `confirmation_waiting`) always launches into an item the app keeps listed until it is answered, the Confirmations panel's pending entry, so the toast is never the only copy; such toasts are never coalesced, and never replaced by another one's. A `job_finished` or `job_failed` toast for jobs whose `origin.surface` isn't `panel` is coalesced per origin: its Tag is `o_<first 16 hex of SHA-256 over the origin's surface and id>`, so a newer one from the same origin replaces the older one in the notification centre instead of stacking, and each pops once, when its batch ends. Confirmation records gain an optional `group: {task_id, gate_id?}`, returned by `app/confirmations.list`; plan 4a never sets it (the director plan does).
  - **Toast XML** (P4a-R8, P4a-R9): `<toast launch="URL" activationType="protocol"><visual><binding template="ToastGeneric"><text>TITLE</text><text>BODY</text></binding></visual>ACTIONS</toast>`, where ACTIONS is `<actions><action content="Open" arguments="<scheme>:confirm/<id>" activationType="protocol"/><action content="Deny" arguments="<scheme>:deny/<id>/<token>" activationType="protocol"/></actions>` for `confirmation_waiting` (P4a-R9) and empty otherwise; TITLE and BODY are `lil_parse::shown_text` output (C0 and C1 controls removed; U+200E, U+200F, U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069, U+200B–U+200D, U+2060 and U+FEFF shown as `⟨U+XXXX⟩`, except a U+200D inside an emoji ZWJ sequence, which is kept: the element before it is an `Extended_Pictographic` code point, optionally followed by one `Emoji_Modifier` (U+1F3FB–U+1F3FF) or by U+FE0F, and the code point after it is `Extended_Pictographic`; every other U+200D is shown (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-24: follows UTS #51 emoji ZWJ sequences); at most 200 `char`s over the whole text, ending `…` when cut), then XML-escaped. `Tag` is the job, batch or confirmation id, a coalesced toast's `o_<16 hex>`, or the restart hold's `restart_<first held job id>`; `Group` is the category.
