A quiet toast has `SuppressPopup = true`. No toast approves anything (owner decision 2026-10-22). (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) After `shown_text`, the characters XML 1.0 doesn't allow (U+FFFE and U+FFFF) are removed, so no text can make a toast unpostable. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough) Every toast's TITLE and BODY, whatever its category (a batch's end, a confirmation, a cap alert, the restart hold, an agent's notice), go through the diagnostics bundle's redaction (Appendix G "Diagnostics"), with the same names, profile folder and computer names, so a job's error message never puts a secret on screen or in Windows' notification history, which the suite can't clear. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough review and re-review) The TITLE and BODY are each redacted in three steps, so that both what came and what the toast shows are covered: (1) the text is redacted as it came, so a terminal control sequence still bounds a key or is removed from inside one, and a key the cut would run through is redacted whole; (2) the Unicode `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` characters are removed, except the bidirectional controls `shown_text` shows as markers (U+200E, U+200F, U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069) and the joiners of an emoji (a U+200D between pictographs, a variation selector right after a pictograph, tag characters after a pictograph), so none can split a key (in a toast, U+200B, U+200C, U+2060 and U+FEFF are therefore removed, not shown); the text is then normalised without the cut (`shown_text`, then the characters XML 1.0 doesn't allow removed), so a control character inside a key (BEL, NUL, NEL, DEL) goes and the key is whole, and redacted, again until normalising and redaction both leave it as it is, (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, PR #53 audit) each normalising followed by taking into a `<redacted>` the run of 8 or more of `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `_` and `-` (and any `[`…`m` left of an SGR) glued right after it, which only a removed splitter can have left there; (3) it is cut last, which only removes text, and normalised and redacted once more until both leave it as it is. A text that hasn't settled after 4 rounds of step 2 or 3 is shown as `<redacted>`. A key split between the TITLE and the BODY is a known limit: each is redacted on its own. The launch and action URLs are not redacted.
  - **Copy:** `job_finished`: "Job finished" (or "Batch finished (<n> jobs)") / "<kind> · <project, or no project> · <elapsed>", launching `<scheme>:task/<job_id>`; `job_failed`: "Job failed" / "<kind>: <error message>", the same launch; `confirmation_waiting`: "Confirmation waiting" / "<who> wants to run <tool>" (who: "Claude Code", "The assistant" or "A script (lil)"), or for a cap alone "A call would go over your <cap> cap", and (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough) for a call that needs both "<who> wants to run <tool>, over your <cap> cap", launching `<scheme>:confirm/<id>`; `cap_alert`: "Spending at <spent> % of your <cap> cap (<period>)" / "Alert at <pct> %" (owner decision 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough: one toast per threshold crossed, each showing the spend as it is: `<spent>` is the alert event's `spent_pct`, so one 2 USD call against a 1 USD cap posts three toasts, each "Spending at 200 % of your monthly cap (2026-10)", with the bodies "Alert at 50 %", "Alert at 80 %" and "Alert at 100 %"; (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough review) for a cap of 0 the title reads "Spent <spent> <currency> against your 0 <currency> <cap> cap (<period>)", such as "Spent 2 USD against your 0 USD monthly cap (2026-10)"); `agent_activity`: "Claude Code" / the message, or for an agent's `open_in` or `ui.show` "<who>" / "<who> opened <target title>" (who as for `confirmation_waiting`; plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25), launching `<scheme>:open/<base64url>` (left out, as for a toast with no target, when that URL would pass 8,192 characters, the longest argument a `lil:` URL may be). (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) A batch's toast counts the newest attempt at each `index` and leaves out cancelled jobs: it is posted once they are all final, and not at all when every one was cancelled; a job a core start or stop marked `interrupted` never ends a batch's toast (the restart hold has its own). With one job counted it reads "Job finished". It launches the batch's first job, and `job_failed` its first failed job, whose error message is the body; `<elapsed>` runs from the first job's start (else its creation) to the last one's end, written "45 s", "3 min 4 s" or "1 h 5 min". A coalesced Tag hashes the UTF-8 of `<surface>:<id>` (`<surface>` alone without an `id`); a `panel` batch's Tag is its `batch_id`, a `cap_alert`'s is `cap_<first 16 hex of SHA-256 over "<cap>:<period>:<pct>">`, and each `agent_activity` toast's a new `n_<16 hex>`. `<who>` is "LILITH" for a `ui:*` caller (`asset.trash`, CORE-157). A `notify.claude_code` toast's TITLE is "Claude Code", or "Claude Code: <title>" with a `title`. It and a `cap_alert` have no target, so they have no `launch` attribute, and a click only dismisses them. The restart hold's body for one job reads "1 job waits until you resume it in Jobs".
  - **Progress** (owner decision 2026-10-24; APP-146): no live progress toasts. A job's progress is shown in the Jobs panel, and toasts go out only for finished work (`job_finished`, `job_failed`) and for what needs you (`confirmation_waiting`, `cap_alert`, the restart hold above, and later `director_decision`). No toast is ever updated in place: a coalesced toast is replaced by a new post. The `job_progress` category of the earlier contract is withdrawn: `DesktopSettings.notifications` has no such key, and an old `job_progress` key in `suite.toml` is kept and ignored, like any unknown key. (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23: a second pop was seen while a progress toast updated on one Windows build, and a `SuppressPopup` toast was seen to pop on another, possibly the control toast posted just before; the spike's FAIL path.)
  - **Quiet while fullscreen:** while the newest poll says fullscreen and `quiet_while_fullscreen` is on, every toast has `SuppressPopup = true`; none is dropped, and confirmations are made quiet like the rest.
  - **Reachable** (ARCH-055, CORE-113, APP-051, QA-016): the notification path can reach you when `notification_path` is `ready`, `ok` and `ok` and `confirmation_waiting` is on, re-read at most every 10 s (P4a-R33). A call that needs a confirmation then gets `pending_confirmation` with no app connected; a non-interactive caller still gets `confirm.unavailable` or `cap.exceeded`. When the path isn't reachable and no app is connected, the call gets `confirm.unavailable` or `cap.exceeded` at once, so a confirmation never waits where you can't see it; with an app connected, it waits there with the top bar's confirmation badge, whatever the toasts' state. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) While the cached reading is `not_yet_shown`, Reachable reads the path again on each call instead of serving the cache, so its end as `no_platform` (5 s after the first post, "Ready" above) answers the next call at once; every other reading is served for 10 s.
  - **Deny from a toast** (P4a-R9): `app/confirmation.answer` takes an optional `toast_token` (with `desktop.v1` in the shared capabilities). With it, only `answer: "deny"` and no `cap` are accepted (`params.invalid` otherwise). A token is live only while its confirmation is pending: answering it by any route (the window, a toast, expiry, cancel) and a core stop kill it at once. The core checks the token first: an unknown, dead or reused token is `confirm.unknown`, whatever state the confirmation is in, so an old toast's Deny after an answer elsewhere, an expiry or a core restart is `confirm.unknown`, and the app shows nothing and logs the outcome (with the token cut). `confirm.already_answered` and `confirm.expired` belong to the window's path only: a live id answered without a token. A deny from a toast is recorded `confirmed_via: app` like any app answer, and the toast is removed.
  - `notify.claude_code {message, title?}` (`write`, module `interchange`): `message` 1–2,000 characters, `title` at most 100; → `{posted, reason?: "category_off" | "foreground" | "duplicate" | "unavailable"}`; the same message within 10 s is a `duplicate`.
- **Activation and registration** (APP-020–022; P4a-R2, P4a-R26):
  - schemes: `lil` (stable), `lil-dev` (dev), `lil-test-<id>` (test channels, desktop group only). URLs: `<scheme>:confirm/<confirmation id>`, `<scheme>:deny/<confirmation id>/<token>`, `<scheme>:task/<job id>`, `<scheme>:open/<base64url of the UTF-8 target, without padding>`, and a raw `lil://` ref.
  - per-user registration under the classes root: `<root>\<scheme>` with `(Default) = "URL:LILITH (<Channel>)"`, an empty `URL Protocol`, `DefaultIcon = "<exe>",0` and `shell\open\command = "<exe>" "%1"`; `<root>\.lilref` with `(Default) = "LILITH.lilref.<channel>"`, and `<root>\LILITH.lilref.<channel>\shell\open\command = "<exe>" "%1"`. The classes root is `HKCU\Software\Classes` for stable and dev and `HKCU\Software\LILITH-test-<id>\Classes` for test channels outside the desktop group; `<exe>` is the channel's `lil-image.exe`. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25) The scheme and the `.lilref` association are checked and written apart: `.lilref` is one association that stable and dev share, so one channel's may be held by the other, and that never hides a missing scheme. `notification_path.handlers` follows the channel's own scheme only, the one toast URLs use: `missing` while it is missing or incomplete, `other_channel` while another exe (another channel's or build's `lil-image.exe`, or another program) holds it, and `ok` when it opens `<exe>`; `.lilref`, missing or held by anyone, never changes it, so toast reachability ("Reachable" above) looks only at the toasts, the shortcut and the scheme. Another channel, build or program holding `.lilref` is only a `lil doctor` `warning:` (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25, Task 13 fix round 1). A `.lilref` naming a file type that isn't `LILITH.lilref.*` is another program's. (plan 4a rulings, 2026-10-25, Task 13 re-review) A scheme or `.lilref` whose holder is a LILITH registration (a `LILITH.lilref.*` file type, or a scheme key whose `(Default)` is `URL:LILITH (<Channel>)`) but whose `lil-image.exe` no longer exists is stale: `missing` in `notification_path`, a `lil doctor` `problem:` naming the dead path, and rewritten for this channel after `y`; it is never deleted. A scheme key with no command is `missing` only when it is LILITH's own (no `(Default)`, or `URL:LILITH (<Channel>)`); one with another `(Default)` is another program's. On stable and dev, a scheme or `.lilref` that another program registered only under `HKLM\Software\Classes` is `other_channel`: the doctor warns and never shadows it with a per-user write.
  - writing them: `lil doctor` after `y`, or the app's "Set up" button (P4a-R26) (owner decision 2026-10-22), through `lil_platform::{write_shortcut, register_handlers}`, and `register_scheme` or `register_lilref` when only one of the two is written (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25);
  - the AUMID shortcut: `<Programs>\LILITH.lnk` for stable, `<Programs>\LILITH (<Channel>).lnk` otherwise, targeting the channel's `lil-image.exe`, with `System.AppUserModel.ID` = `LILITH.Suite.<Channel>` and the CLSID below; `<Programs>` is the per-user Start-menu Programs folder (`FOLDERID_Programs`), or `<test root>\profile\Programs\` on a test channel.
  - the CLSID (plan 4a result, 2026-10-23): the shortcut also carries `System.AppUserModel.ToastActivatorCLSID`, a CLSID fixed per channel, registered per user as `<root>\CLSID\{<CLSID>}\LocalServer32 = "<exe>"` under the channel's classes root, and written, checked and removed with the shortcut; `notification_path.shortcut` is `wrong` without it. Spike 11's rule adds the CLSID when a test in the notification centre itself fails or can't run, and on the test PC the centre can't be read (UI Automation saw no toast in it), so it is built from the start instead of after such a test. It only keeps a missed toast in the notification centre: every toast activates by protocol, and no COM activator runs.
  - the AUMID's registration (plan 4a ruling 2026-10-24): with the shortcut, the channel also registers its AUMID per user as `<root>\AppUserModelId\<AUMID>` under the channel's classes root (`HKCU\Software\Classes` for stable and dev and for the desktop group's test channels on the shell's root), with `DisplayName` = `LILITH` for stable and `LILITH (<Channel>)` otherwise, `IconUri` when the channel has an icon file, and `CustomActivator` = the channel's CLSID; it is written, checked and removed with the shortcut, and `notification_path.shortcut` is `wrong` without it. Why: S1.2 recorded that a toast posts and shows with only this key and no shortcut, and the desktop group's host run found a freshly written shortcut's AUMID unknown to `ToastNotifier::Setting()` (`Element not found`) for over 60 s, so the key makes a new install's first toast work before the Start-menu index has the shortcut. On a test channel every key the registration writes also carries the value `LilTestChannel` = the test channel's id, so a killed run's leftovers are found by it (Task 30's sweep).
- **`lil:` URLs** (APP-020, QA-050; P4a-R3), parsed by `lil_parse::parse_lil_url`:
  - the input is one command-line argument from the raw command line (`GetCommandLineW`), with surrounding spaces and one pair of surrounding `"` removed; at most 8,192 characters;
  - the scheme (up to the first `:`) is compared ASCII case-insensitively; `lil://` and a ref is a ref under every channel; `confirm/`, `deny/`, `task/` and `open/` are accepted only under the channel's own scheme;
  - `confirm/<id>` needs `^cf_[0-9a-f]{16}$`, `deny/<id>/<token>` needs that id and `^[0-9a-f]{32}$`, `task/<id>` needs `^j_[0-9a-f]{16}$`, and `open/<b64>` needs base64url without `=`, decoding to 1–4,096 bytes of UTF-8 with no control character, which must parse as a ref when it starts with `lil://`; nothing may follow except one trailing `/`, which is dropped;
  - a raw ref has its `%HH` escapes decoded once as UTF-8 (refusing `%00`–`%1F`, `%7F`, invalid UTF-8 and a decoded `%`), then must parse as a ref;
  - anything else is refused with "This link isn't one LILITH opens: <shown_text of the argument, at most 80 characters>", and nothing happens;
  - in plan 4a (P4a-R21) `confirm` opens the confirm window; `deny` opens no window and takes no focus: the running app, or else the confirm-window process started without a window, sends the deny and exits (P4a-R9, P4a-R11); `task` opens the Jobs panel on that job, and `open` and a raw ref show the target in Home's "Opened" card;
  - `open_in` and `ui.show` (the plan-3 carry N6): `launch_command.args` is the registered `args`, then `--intent <intent>`, then `<scheme>:open/<base64url(target)>`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) So that every such URL is one the handler accepts, a target of `handlers.for`, `open_in` and `ui.show` is at most 4,096 bytes of UTF-8 (`params.invalid` otherwise), the longest an `open/` URL carries.
- **`.lilref` files** (APP-021): an argument whose path ends `.lilref` (any case) is read, at most 4 KiB, with `lil_schema::parse_lilref`, and opened as its ref. `lilref.write {ref, title?}` (`write`, module `interchange`) writes `<local dir>\cache\lilref\<first 16 hex of SHA-256 of the ref>.lilref` and returns `{path}`; the ref must be a sealed ref carrying `#sha256=`, else `ref.invalid`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) The ref is hashed and written in its canonical text, the file is `lil_schema::write_lilref`'s, a `title` that `parse_lilref` would refuse (over 200 characters, or a control or text-direction character) is `params.invalid`, and writing the same ref again replaces its file; nothing is ever written outside `<local dir>\cache\lilref\`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25, Task 11 review) The suite's cache writer (`.lilref` files and the diagnostics folder) refuses a file name holding `:` (a stream), `*?"<>|` or a control character, one ending in `.` or a space, and a reserved device name (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `CONIN$`, `CONOUT$`, `COM0`–`COM9`, `COM¹`–`COM³`, `LPT0`–`LPT9`, `LPT¹`–`LPT³`, with any extension); it removes the old file only once the new one is in place: when a replace fails halfway it moves the old file back, and keeps it at its backup name if that fails too; and it removes its own temp files (`.<name>.<pid>-<n>.tmp`) older than a day from the folder it writes, nothing else. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) That sweep never removes a backup file (`.<name>.<pid>-<n>.bak`): one is left only when a failed replace couldn't move the old file back, and it is then that file's only copy. A sharing violation is retried with the store's backoff (10 s in all), and a temp name a crashed core left behind is skipped for the next one.
- **Apps** (APP-002, APP-013, APP-014, APP-111):
  - single instance: the running instance writes `{"app": "<app>", "pid": <pid>}` when a client connects; the second launch checks the server the pipe reports (ARCH-027: its image is this executable, its token user is this user, its integrity level is no lower than this launch's and its token is neither restricted nor an AppContainer's), and writes nothing to it unless it is another process that didn't start after this launch opened the pipe: a real creator can't be newer than that, while one launch of a burst may well start before the launch that claims the pipe. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-30, PR #65 audit) `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` names the pipe's creator even after that process has died and the handle has passed to another, so a reused pid can pass those checks; the holder must therefore prove itself before it gets any argument. The second launch creates a fresh event with a random 128-bit name in the session's `Local\` namespace and default security, `Local\lil-launch-proof-<32 hex>`, and writes `{"prove": "<name>"}`; the running instance opens it with `EVENT_MODIFY_STATE`, sets it, and answers `{"proved": true}` (`false` when it can't, and it opens no other name); the second launch goes on only when the event is set. A process at a lower integrity level can't get that right (no write up) and an AppContainer can't reach the name; a same-user process at the same or a higher level is already outside the threat model (ARCH-027), a write-restricted token at that level whose restricting SIDs include this user among them. The second launch then calls `AllowSetForegroundWindow(pid)` (not for a deny or `--no-activate`), writes `{"args": [<argument>, …]}`, reads `{"ok": true}` and exits 0, and the running instance then brings its window to the front with `SetForegroundWindow`. Each message is one JSON line of at most 64 KiB, within 2 s. A launch whose exchange fails, however it fails, never ends silently: it opens its own window with a banner naming the cause (a deny sends itself and shows no window, P4a-R9), so a LILITH of this user in another Windows session, which can't reach the session's `Local\` name, gets a window of its own too. A refused link too long for the message is cut to its first 8 KiB and `…` before it goes on the wire, and still reaches the Opened card, refused. The confirm window's pipe is `\\.\pipe\lilith-app-<app>-confirm-<userSID>-<channel>` (P4a-R11).
  - command line: `lil-image [--intent open | edit | use_as_input] [--no-activate] [--confirm [<id>]] [<URL> | <path>]`; `--confirm` with no id lists every pending confirmation; `--no-activate` shows the window without activating it and flashes its taskbar button. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26) `--smoke-exit`, for tests only, quits with 0 once the window's first frame is shown and the event loop has gone idle.
  - start (APP-020, APP-137; P4a-R3) (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A, as ruled at Task 15's review): before Qt is initialised, an app (a) restricts DLL searches to its own folder and System32 (`SetDefaultDllDirectories(LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_APPLICATION_DIR | LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32)`, then `SetDllDirectoryW(L"")`), so a DLL planted on `PATH` or in the current folder never loads; (b) refuses a command line that isn't its grammar above (until the single-instance and activation work, plan 4a Task 19, adds the rest, the image app takes only `--no-activate` and `--smoke-exit`, each at most once, and refuses everything else, a URL or path included); (c) removes from its environment every variable whose name starts with `QT_`, `QML_`, `QML2_`, `QSG_`, `QV4_`, `QTWEBENGINE` or `VK_LOADER_`, and `VK_INSTANCE_LAYERS`, `VK_LAYER_PATH`, `VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH`, `VK_ICD_FILENAMES` and `VK_DRIVER_FILES` (names compared without regard to case), and checks them gone; (d) sets its own folder as Qt's only library path, beside a `qt.conf` that pins Qt's other folders to it; and (e) builds its `QGuiApplication` from the program name alone, so no Qt option reaches Qt, with QML debugging compiled out. If a step fails, or the app can't tell which folder it runs from, the start is refused: exit 2, before the application object exists. "Before Qt is initialised" means before the application object and any Qt call of `main`; Qt's DLLs and the app's registration statics run their static initialisers earlier, and none of them may read the environment.
  - app state (P4a-R16): `<local dir>\apps\<app>\state.json` = `{"state": 1, "workspaces": {"<workspace id>": {"last_seen_at": "<RFC 3339>"}}}`; `<local dir>\apps\<app>\layout.json` = `{"layout": 1, "windows": [{"name", "geometry": {"x", "y", "w", "h"}, "maximized", "screen": {"name", "scale"}}], "kddw": <KDDockWidgets' saved layout as JSON>}` (APP-002). A file that doesn't parse or doesn't restore is renamed to `<name>.bad-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>`, and the app uses the Default layout.
  - "While you were away" (APP-111): the jobs of `jobs.list {since: last_seen_at}` that finished or failed, and those in `interrupted_unknown`, plus the jobs held by a restart (P4a-R41) and the pending confirmations; `last_seen_at` becomes now once Home has been visible for 3 s.
- **Session and listing tools** (ARCH-030, ARCH-088, APP-006, APP-043): `app/set_context {project: <slug> | null}` → `{workspace: {id, path} | null, project, effective_project}` (`project.unknown` for an unknown slug); the workspace is chosen by the session's `core/hello`, and the app switches it with a new hello (`Session::set_workspace`, P4a-R34), never by changing the default; `projects.list {}` (`read`, module `projects`) → `{projects: [{project, name, status, assets}]}`; `workspaces.list {}` (`read`, module `workspace`) → `{workspaces: [{id, path, name?, default, served, missing}]}`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) `app/set_context` with a slug makes it the session's project as a call naming it does (and so the hint, on a read-write workspace, ARCH-088); with `null` it forgets the session's project, so its calls fall back to the hint, then `sketchbook`, and it answers `project: null`; a slug in a session without a workspace is `workspace.required`. `projects.list` lists each `projects/<slug>/` that holds a `project.yaml`, by slug: `name` is its `name` (else the slug), `status` as `project.status` reads it, and `assets` the number of its assets in the index. `workspaces.list` lists `default_workspace` and each `[[workspaces]]` entry once, in `suite.toml`'s order with the default first: `id` is the hash of the canonical folder (ARCH-030), or of the path as registered when the folder is gone (`missing: true`), `name` is the workspace file's `name`, and `served` marks the workspace this core serves (in M1, the default). (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25, Task 11 review) `app/set_context {}` (no `project`) changes nothing and answers the session's workspace and the project the session has set, or `project: null` when it has set none (never the hint or `sketchbook` it falls back to); only `null` forgets the project. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-30, Task 20 review) `effective_project` is in every answer: the project a call naming none would use in this session now (the session's, else the hint, else `sketchbook`), `null` in a session without a workspace or when its workspace can't be opened. Reading it writes nothing, so it never pins the fallback or changes the hint; `project` stays what the session has set. It is true when read: another client can change the hint and a call naming a project changes the session's, with no event for either. A registered path where a file is, rather than a folder, is listed with `missing: false` and `problem: "not_a_folder"`, and one that can't be resolved for another reason than being gone with `problem: "unreadable"`; `missing: true` means only that nothing is there. A `suite.toml` that can't be read or isn't valid makes `workspaces.list` fail with `store.io` (no `suite.toml` lists none), and a `projects/` folder that can't be read makes `projects.list` fail with `store.io`; one project whose file can't be read is listed with `status: "unknown"`.
- **Connections** (APP-029, APP-041, ARCH-021, ARCH-029; plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A):
  - an app's own cap prompts: a `ui:*` session's pending cap confirmations are cancelled when its app's process exits, not when one of its connections closes, so a reconnect keeps them (APP-029: "if the app closes first"). A later connection of the same process (the same client pid and caller kind) in the same workspace finds them still pending: it can answer them, and a retry with their `retry_token` gets the same `pending_confirmation`. A connection of that process in another workspace (a switch) cancels at once those of the workspace it left, whichever of the two connections closes or opens first. A process the core can't open at the hello counts as gone when its connection closes. An app's prompts left pending meanwhile are answerable as any other confirmation (the Confirmations panel, a toast), and they still expire after 30 minutes. This holds within one core instance: after a core restart, the new core has no process to wait on, and the prompts it loads end at their 30-minute expiry;
  - a draining core answers `core/hello` with `core.draining` and starts no session (the connection keeps waiting for a hello, ARCH-029); `lil core stop` takes that answer as a core already stopping. Compatibility: a `lil core stop` built before this clarification (protocol 1.2) that runs while the core drains reports the `core.draining` refusal instead of waiting, and the core stops all the same. A stop doesn't keep a core away from open apps: once the stopped core's pipe is gone, an app's session starts a new core as ARCH-018 says;
  - `app/*` methods are admitted as `tools/call` is (APP-041): a draining core answers them `core.draining`, each counts as a call in flight until it is answered, and each error outcome is one of the core's recent errors (`diagnostics.recent`), with the method as its tool.
- **`lil-mcp` launches** (ARCH-015, APP-065): when an agent's `open_in` or `ui.show` result has `running: false` and a `launch_command` whose `exe` is a suite app registered beside the core (`apps\<app>.toml`), `lil-mcp` starts it detached, with `--no-activate` before the URL and without focus (`SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE`), and adds the line "Started <app> in the background." to the result text. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) Only a session the core gave caller kind `mcp` launches: a `lil-mcp` that isn't Claude Code's direct child is `cli` (ARCH-047) and, like `lil call`, only returns the `launch_command`. "Beside the core" is the folder of the core process the session reached (`lil_status`'s `core.pid`), which must be a folder the client trusts (ARCH-027); the `exe` is compared with each `apps\<app>.toml` there (a relative `exe` is beside the core), never with an external program's registration in `%APPDATA%\LILITH\apps\`. When the start fails, the line is "Could not start <app> in the background: <why>." instead. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25, Task 11 review) The launched arguments are the matched installed registration's own `args`, then `--intent <open|edit|use_as_input>`, `--no-activate` and one `<channel scheme>:open/<base64url>` URL; they are never the core's `args` passed through, and a `launch_command` whose `args` are anything other than that registration's `args`, `--intent <intent>` and such a URL starts nothing. The core skips an external registration whose `exe` is in the core's folder or below it, or is the same file as a suite program (an `.exe` in the core's folder or an installed registration's `exe`) by volume serial and file id, as a hard link, an 8.3 name or a UNC path to a local share is, so no external file can start a suite program with arguments of its own. The one exception is the test app (`lil-test-app.exe`) on a test channel, whose core accepts it as `ui:test` (QA-066); `lil-mcp` never launches it, since it is no installed registration.
- **Plugin 1.3.1** (ARCH-103, AI-116; P4a-R18): `hooks/hooks.json` gains `Notification` with the exec-form hook `{"type": "command", "command": "${user_config.lil_path}", "args": ["hook", "notification"]}`, with no `onFailure` field: Claude Code ignores a Notification hook's exit code and output, so `"block"` would block nothing and only mislead, and the field needs Claude Code 2.1.295 or later; `plugin.json` has `version: "1.3.1"`. `lil hook notification` reads the hook JSON (`message`, `title?`) and calls `notify.claude_code` without starting a core; it prints nothing and exits 0. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) A `message` over 2,000 characters or a `title` over 100 is cut to that length; an input without a non-empty `message` calls nothing, and so does Engine A (`LIL_ENGINE=1`, ARCH-107). (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25, Task 11 review) The hook waits at most 5 s for the core, then exits.
- **Diagnostics** (ARCH-117–122, QA-082–085; P4a-R19, P4a-R20):
  - `diagnostics.bundle {job_id?}` (`write`, module `diagnostics`) writes `<local dir>\diagnostics\lilith-diagnostics-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>.zip` → `{path, bytes, files: [string], redactions}`. Entries: `manifest.json` (`{bundle: 1, created, build_id, channel, protocol}`), `versions.json` (the core, each app that connected, ComfyUI, the Qt pair, KDDockWidgets, the driver), `suite.toml` (redacted), `errors.json` (`diagnostics.recent` with limit 200), `hardware.json` (`Snapshot`), `engines.json` (`engines.status` and `engines.environment`), `logs\<file>` (the newest 5 MiB of each process's newest log file) and, with `job_id`, `job.json` (its `JobSummary` and tool arguments, untrusted). Never a workspace file. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) Entry names use `/`, as zip files do (`logs/<file>`), and `files` lists them so; a log is cut at a line start; `suite.toml` is left out when there is none. `versions.json`'s apps are those that said hello to this core, each with the `build_id` of its `core/hello`; protocol 1.2 carries no Qt or KDDockWidgets version, so the Qt pair and KDDockWidgets are `null` until a later protocol adds them. `job.json` is `{job, tool, arguments, untrusted: true}`, with `tool` and `arguments` `null` for a job no tool call made; an unknown `job_id` is `job.unknown`. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25) A bundle never replaces another: a name that is taken (a bundle of the same second) becomes `lilith-diagnostics-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>-2.zip`, `-3` and on, taken by a move that never replaces, never by checking first, trying numbers until one is free. No bundle replaces another, and no path is handed out twice: a name this core handed out already is skipped too. The diagnostics folder then keeps the newest 10 bundles, ordered by time stamp and then number (compared as a number), and never removes one whose file was made less than 60 s ago, judged by the file system's creation time and not by the time stamp in its name, nor the one whose file was created most recently before the new bundle's, whatever the clocks and the names say, so a burst keeps all its bundles and no bundle removes the one made just before it; a name whose time stamp isn't digits is no bundle and is never removed. The logs hold at most 32 MiB in all, `lil-core`'s first; a log whose file name a redaction rule changes is named `logs/redacted-<n>.<date>.log`. A recent error's message is kept up to 16,384 characters and shown up to 2,000, each cut back to the last space or separator before the limit so no word is cut in two; in a bundle it is redacted whole first and cut only then.
  - Redaction: every value under a key holding one of Appendix F's scrub words (`KEY`, `TOKEN`, `SECRET`, `PASSWORD`, `PASSWD`, `CREDENTIAL`, `AUTH`), every Claude Code profile label in `suite.toml`, every e-mail address (`[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}`) and every Credential Manager target becomes `<redacted>`; and (owner decision 2026-10-22) the user-profile folder becomes `%USERPROFILE%` and the computer name `<machine>`. `redactions` counts the replacements. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) The profile folder is matched in its long form and its 8.3 short form, in any letter case, with `\` or `/` separators, with a `\\?\` prefix, and JSON-escaped with doubled backslashes; the computer name in any letter case and in its machine-id spelling (ARCH-081). In text other than `suite.toml`'s TOML (the logs and the JSON entries), a key is a run of letters, digits, `_`, `-` and `.` (optionally quoted) followed by `=` or `:` (not `::`, and not a `.rs` file's line number), and its value the quoted string, bracketed JSON or word after it (two words after `Bearer`, `Basic`, `Digest` or `Token`). (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) In a `Debug` or tuple pair whose quoted first element holds a scrub word (`("API_KEY", "…")`, escaped or not), the second element is redacted too, when it is a quoted string. A Credential Manager target is `LILITH` or the suite's name before the rename, an optional `-<channel>`, then `/<name>`, in any letter case. A Deny URL's token (`deny/<confirmation id>/<token>`) becomes `<redacted>` too. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25) These are redacted too, wherever they are: a Windows SID (`S-1-<n>-<n>…`, pipe names included) becomes `<sid>`; well-known secret formats with no key before them (GitHub `ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghu_`, `ghs_`, `ghr_` and `github_pat_` tokens, Hugging Face `hf_`, `sk-ant-` and `sk-` keys with a long tail, `AGE-SECRET-KEY-1`, Slack `xox[abpr]-`, AWS `AKIA` key ids and JWT-shaped tokens) become `<redacted>` where a word starts, and (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough) glued to a word too (`j_sk-ant-…`) when the body after its prefix is 32 characters or more and, (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A walkthrough review) looks like a secret: at least two digits and two letters, and one part between `-` and `_` of 16 characters or more holding both letters and digits (a JWT glued to a word needs only its length). So a hyphenated name such as `task-generate-character-portraits-batch-01` or `risk-assessment-…` is not taken for a key, though a long mixed run of letters and digits glued after `sk-`, `hf_` or `gh?_` may be (over-redaction is safe), and an AWS `AKIA` id, whose body is 16 characters, goes only where a word starts. Each body is read once, so the work grows linearly with the text; (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, PR #53 audit) a well-known token's `<redacted>` also takes the rest of the token when one splitter cut it: a run of 8 or more of `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `_` and `-` right after it, across exactly one splitter (a control character, CR LF counting as one; its written escape after any run of backslashes, such as `\n`, `\r\n`, `\t`, `\e`, `\u0007`, `\u001b` or `\x07`; a terminal control sequence, raw or written, that the rules remove; or an invisible character, a `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` other than the bidirectional controls; (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, PR #53 re-audit) also U+FFFE or U+FFFF; two splitters in a row, raw or written, such as `\n\n`, `\u0007\u0007` or two SGRs, count as one), and any `[`…`m` an SGR left between them; (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, PR #53 re-audit) a well-known prefix whose body one such splitter cuts before the format's shortest body (for a JWT, before its shortest form of three parts) goes too, with the part before the splitter and the run of the body's characters right after it, when the two together reach that shortest body or form, however short the run after it is; a word after a space is never taken; an `open/` payload under a suite scheme (a path, ref or name, also after `//` as in `lil://open/` and with an escaped `\/`) becomes `open/<redacted>` whether it decodes or not, over `[A-Za-z0-9+/=_-]` (its `%2B`, `%2F` and `%3D` decoded first); a Deny URL's token goes with its `/` escaped (`\/`) too; and the account name (the profile folder's last part, 4 characters or more) becomes `<user>`. A scrub-word key's value also covers a `Debug` value such as `Some("…")` or `Secret("…")` to its closing parenthesis, a string escaped any number of times, and, for a flag holding a scrub word (`--api-key`), the next argument, space-separated or the next element of an array. A known literal (the account name, the computer names, the labels, each profile's `config_dir` and the profile folder) of 6 characters or more is replaced wherever it appears, inside a word too, since over-redaction is safe; a shorter one only as a whole word. Labels, `config_dir` and the profile folder are compared after normalising: `/` and `\` are the same, a run of them counts as one, and letter case is ignored; a `config_dir` is matched without a separator at its end and without a `\\?\` prefix (`\\?\UNC\` standing for `\\`). No literal is matched inside a placeholder (`%USERPROFILE%`, `<redacted>`, `<user>`, `<machine>`, `<sid>`). Every rule's test that a value isn't inside a word counts an escape right before it as a boundary: `\n`, `\r`, `\t`, `\b`, `\f`, `\v`, `\a`, `\e`, `\0`, `\xXX`, `\uXXXX` and `\UXXXXXXXX` (after any run of backslashes), `%XX` (decoded or not), `&#NN;` and `&#xHH;`. A SID goes whatever follows it; the computer name's DNS host name is matched as well as `COMPUTERNAME`; a quoted Credential Manager target runs to its quote. The rules run over the text as it came; then, when it holds terminal control sequences, these are removed, since they carry nothing a bundle needs, and the rules run over it again, so the removal never undoes what the first round caught. Removed are a CSI (`ESC [`, parameter and intermediate bytes, then a final byte from 0x40 to 0x7E (never a `"` or a `\`), or up to a placeholder the first round left there); an OSC (`ESC ]` to its terminator: after a raw `ESC` a raw BEL or `ESC \`, after a written one `\u0007`, `\x07` or a written `ESC \` of the same escape level, never `\a`, which in log text is a backslash and a letter, as in `D:\assets`); and, after a raw `ESC`, a 2-byte sequence whose second byte is `7`, `8`, `=`, `>`, `c`, `D`, `E`, `H`, `M`, `N`, `O`, `P`, `Z` or `\` (before any other byte the `ESC` goes alone). The `ESC` is the raw byte, or `\u001b`, `\x1b` or `\u{1b}` after any run of backslashes; `\e`, `\033`, `%1B` and `&#27;` are an `ESC` only before a whole CSI, so a path such as `D:\exports` is never touched. Nothing is put in their place, so a value a sequence split is whole again, and the place where one stood is a word boundary; so is the place after `ESC [` and parameter bytes where a CSI's final byte would go. Before the rules, `%5C`, `%2F`, `%3A`, `%40`, `%20`, `%2B`, `%3D` and `%25`, percent-encoded UTF-8, the `\u` escapes (after any run of backslashes) and the `&#NN;` and `&#xHH;` forms of those characters and of non-ASCII ones are decoded, a backslash staying escaped; the decoding and the rules run again until nothing changes, at most 3 times, so a value encoded twice is redacted too. A TOML multi-line string (`'''` or `"""`) under a scrub-word key runs to its closing delimiter in any text or, never closed, to the end of its own line and no further. A `suite.toml` that doesn't parse loses a redacted key's whole multi-line value. Known limits, documented rather than redacted: a value split across lines (its key on one line and its value on the next, as Python's `pprint` writes a long dict) is redacted line by line, so the value isn't seen as the key's; (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, PR #53 audit) this is narrower for well-known tokens: one cut by one splitter inside one entry or line (a JSON log line's `\n`, a toast) goes whole when the part after the splitter follows it directly and either is 8 characters or more or, (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, PR #53 re-audit) with the part before it, reaches the format's shortest body, so a key cut before its shortest body loses both parts; what stays is a part after a space or an indent; a part of 7 characters or fewer after a cut past the shortest body; a part after a second cut further on, except that when the first cut came before the shortest body and the parts before the second cut reach it, a part of 8 characters or more after the second cut goes too and only a part after a third cut stays; every part of a key cut twice whose first two parts together don't reach its shortest body; the part after three or more splitters in a row inside a key (every part, when they cut it before its shortest body); (in a toast, these cuts count only when written splitters make them, since a toast removes the raw ones, which joins the parts); in the bundle and the recent errors, the key character a raw lone `ESC` takes when the two form a 2-byte sequence (an `ESC` before `7`, `8`, `=`, `>`, `c`, `D`, `E`, `H`, `M`, `N`, `O`, `P`, `Z` or `\`), so an exact-length id such as an AWS `AKIA` id cut that way before its 16th character stays, short by that character; no producer in this suite writes three splitters in a row or a lone `ESC` inside a key; and, for one split across a file's own lines, the part on the next line; a bidirectional control (U+200E, U+200F, U+202A-U+202E, U+2066-U+2069) inside a token is not a splitter, so the part after it stays, and a toast shows the control as its marker between the two readable parts, on purpose (such a control only gets inside a key on purpose); and a known literal shorter than 6 characters (an account name of 4 or 5 characters, a short computer name or label) is matched only as a whole word, so it stays where it is written with no space next to letters, digits or `_` (`ab12_env`), as in Chinese, Japanese or Thai text (`用户ab12登录`, `ユーザーab12`), or right after an escape the list above doesn't name (an octal `\101`, `\cX`, `\N{…}`); and a value a rule matches in part before a terminal control sequence splits it (a token, SID, e-mail address, `open/` payload, key value or target name) loses that part, but the rest stays; no producer in this suite splits those values (only highlighting part of a value does, as `grep --color` on a part match). A quoted value after a key stays when an OSC or a lone `ESC` stands between them (`api_key=ESC]0;password=BEL"…"`, `api_key=ESC]0;t BEL"…"`, `api_key=ESC'…'`): the first round takes the sequence's head as the key's value; no producer writes such a sequence there. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) A pair whose second element isn't a quoted string (`("TOKEN", Some("…"))`, a number), a pair whose first element isn't quoted, and a name and its value in separate fields (`name: "API_KEY", value: "…"`) keep the value.
  - `diagnostics.recent {limit?}` (`read`, module `diagnostics`; 1–200, default 50) → `{errors: [{code, count, first_at, last_at, last_message, last_tool?}]}`, one row per code, the newest last error first, from the core's last 1,000 error outcomes in memory; the `diagnostics` topic gets `{kind: "error", code, tool?, at}` for each. (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A) A job's final failure (status `failed`, `interrupted` included) is one of those error outcomes, but not a cancel, a job that never started (one a stop left queued), a job left `interrupted_unknown`, or such a job once you resolve it: its row's `last_tool` is the job's kind and `last_job_id?` its id, and its event also carries `job_id`. (review fix, 2026-10-26) Every message kept, a job's or a tool call's, is redacted as a bundle's entries are before it is kept.
  - Logs: every process (`lil-core`, `lil`, `lil-mcp`, each app) writes `<local dir>\logs\<process>.<YYYY-MM-DD>.log` as JSON lines, a new file each day, 14 kept per process (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-26, Checkpoint A: the day's own file is never one of those removed, even when the clock is behind newer files); a day's file stops at 64 MiB with one `log_capped` line; every process installs a panic hook that logs `panic` with its location (ARCH-119); an app's Qt messages are logged with their `qt_category`. Each app call carries a `trace_id` (`tr_<16 hex>`, ARCH-118). (plan 4a ruling, 2026-10-25) The cap counts the file, which several processes of one name may append to (one `lil-mcp` per Claude Code session): each line waits up to 200 ms for the file's append lock and is written after reading the file's length, under the lock or, best effort, without it once the 200 ms have passed, and a process that finds the `log_capped` line writes no more. `lil dev`, the developer tooling, is no suite process and installs neither the log nor the panic hook. The core removes its `core.<YYYY-MM-DD>.log` files from before plan 4a once they are 14 days old.
- **The `lil` commands this plan adds.** Exit codes are as in Appendix D.

  | Command | Output |
  |---|---|
  | `lil confirm-window [<id>]` | starts the channel's `lil-image.exe` from `lil.exe`'s folder with `--confirm [<id>]`, prints `confirm window started (pid <pid>)` and exits 0; with no `lil-image.exe` there, exit 2 and "lil-image.exe isn't beside lil.exe"; with `--non-interactive`, exit 2 and "the confirm window is for interactive use" (P4a-R4). (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) An `<id>` that isn't `cf_` and 16 lowercase hex digits is refused the same way, with exit 2, and nothing is started, so no other argument reaches the app |
  | `lil hook notification` | as in "Plugin 1.3.1" above |
  | `lil doctor` | adds: the AUMID shortcut (`ok:`, or `problem:`, created or repaired only after you type `y`); the channel's scheme and `.lilref` handlers (`ok:`, `problem:` when missing, `warning:` when another channel holds them, written only after `y`); a `warning:` for each `engines.environment` change, ending "Roll back with ComfyUI-Manager's snapshot restore or comfy-cli's version switch; the suite doesn't restore engines (CORE-166)."; the asset catalogue (`ok: ComfyUI's asset catalogue is off`, `warning: ComfyUI's asset catalogue is on`, `info: ComfyUI's asset catalogue is unknown`); and `info:` counts from `models.report`. (plan 4a clarification, 2026-10-25) When another channel or build holds the handlers, the doctor asks nothing and leaves them as they are: only missing handlers are written, after `y`. The ComfyUI lines need `[engines.comfyui]` in `suite.toml` and a running core: without the table they are one `ok:` line saying the checks are skipped, and with no core running the doctor starts none for them (a core that has just started has no engine reading yet) and says so in one `info:` line. (plan 4a rulings, 2026-10-25) The scheme and `.lilref` are reported apart: a missing own scheme is a `problem:`, registered after `y` without touching `.lilref`, even when another channel holds `.lilref`; one held by another channel, build or program is a `warning:` naming its holder, and kept. Outside a test channel, a missing `lil-image.exe` is one `problem:` naming its path, and nothing is offered, so a working shortcut or handler is never pointed at a missing exe. A core the doctor's own workspace checks started in the same run isn't read: one `info:` line says to run the doctor again once the engine is ready. A running core that can't be reached is a `problem:`, and the doctor goes on. Each engine-change `warning:` names the newer capture's day ("changed on <YYYY-MM-DD>"), so its repeat on a later run reads as the same change; the doctor stores nothing about what it has shown |

- **Fail points added:** `jobs.after_withdraw`.
- **Clarifications recorded in Appendix G** (no new rule IDs): ARCH-016, CORE-113 and APP-049, `lil confirm-window`; ARCH-055, CORE-113, APP-051 and QA-016, when the notification path can reach you; APP-020, the activation schemes and the URL grammar; APP-020 and APP-137, how an app starts (its DLL search, environment, library path and argv); APP-022, the shortcut, the registration and the toast XML; ARCH-031, APP-039 and APP-043, `app/desktop.set` and `app/set_context`; APP-065, the agent-activity toast; APP-146, the Claude Code and agent activity category, the withdrawn progress category and the toast rules; CORE-014, `desktop` and `diagnostics` are infrastructure; CORE-117, the new `JobSummary` fields; CORE-130, the stored captures; CORE-146, the new tool rows; CORE-160, the pause reasons, the withdrawal and the 2 s poll; ARCH-022 and CORE-160, the restart hold (a ruled exception, P4a-R41, not a clarification); CORE-161, the history rule and `estimate_key`; CORE-164, the header limits; ARCH-030, switching workspace by a new hello; ARCH-072, the watchdog bound; ARCH-117, the log cap; ARCH-126, the launch-profile keys; APP-063 and the plan-3 carry N6, base64url launch arguments; APP-029, APP-041, ARCH-021 and ARCH-029, an app's cap prompts across reconnects, a hello to a draining core and the admission of `app/*` methods; ARCH-032, the preview topics' history and the history sweep; ARCH-025, `jobs.list` pages under the line cap.
